## The Huldrefish7 / 哈尔德鱼7（huldrefisk）

===Original===
It was such an odd trout that Nona hauled in at the end of his fishing-line. Large and fat, red spotted and shiny, it sprawled and squirmed, with its dirty yellow belly above the water, to wriggle off the hook. And when he got it into the boat, and took it off the hook, he saw that it had only two small slits where the eyes should have been.

It must be a huldrefish, thought one of the boatmen, for rumour had it that that lake was one of those which had a double bottom.

But Nona didn’t trouble his head very much about what sort of a fish it was, so long as it was a big one. He was ravenously hungry, and bawled to them to row as rapidly as possible ashore so as to get it cooked.

He had been sitting the whole afternoon with empty lines out in the mountain lake there; but as for the trout, it was only an hour ago since it had been steering its way through the water with its rudder of a tail, and allowed itself to be fooled by a hook, and already it lay cooked red there on the dish.

===Chinese===
诺纳（Nona）从钓线末端拽上来的，是一条好生古怪的鳟鱼。它又大又肥，红斑点点，鳞光闪闪，肚皮泛着脏黄翻出水面，扑腾扭动，直想甩脱钓钩。待他把鱼拽进船里，从钩上摘下，这才看见那本该长眼睛的地方，只有两道细细的窄缝。

船夫中有一人心想，这准是条哈尔德鱼，因为传闻说，那湖是有着双层湖底的湖泊之一。

可诺纳并不怎么操心这鱼是什么来路，只要个头大就行。他饿得发慌，扯着嗓子喊他们拼命把船往岸边划，好赶紧把鱼煮了吃。

他整个下午都守着空空的钓线，坐在那片山中湖上；至于那条鳟鱼，一个钟头之前还在水里摆着舵一般的尾鳍游动，被一枚鱼钩诓骗上手，这会儿却已红彤彤地煮熟，躺在盘子里了。

===Original===
But now Nona recollected about the strange eyes, and felt for them, and pricked away at its head with his fork. There was nothing but slits outside, and yet there was a sort of hard eyeball inside. The head was strangely shaped, and looked very peculiar in many respects.

He was vexed that he had not examined it more closely before it was cooked; it was not so easy now to make out what it really was. It had tasted first-rate, however, and that was something.

But at night there was, as it were, a gleam of bright water before his eyes, and he lay half asleep, thinking of the odd fish he had pulled up.

He was in his boat again, he thought, and it seemed to him as if his hands felt the fish wriggling and sprawling for its life, and shooting its snout backwards and forwards to get off the hook.

===Chinese===
这时诺纳才想起那双古怪的眼睛，便用手指去摸索，又拿叉子在鱼头上戳戳点点。外头只有一道道细缝，里头却似乎藏着一颗硬硬的眼球。那鱼头形状古怪，处处透着蹊跷。

他懊恼没趁鱼下锅之前细看个明白；如今可不容易弄清它到底是什么了。好在滋味极佳，这也算得一点安慰。

可是到了夜里，他眼前仿佛有一片明亮的水光浮动，他半睡半醒地躺着，思量着自己钓上来的那条怪鱼。

他觉得自己又坐回了船上，那鱼垂死挣扎、上下扑腾的动静似乎还在手上，鱼吻一探一缩，只想着甩脱钓钩。

===Original===
All at once it grew so heavy and strong that it drew the boat after it by the line.

It went along at a frightful speed, while the lake gradually diminished, as it were, and dried up.

There was an irresistible sucking of the water in the direction the fish went, which was towards a hole at the bottom of the lake like a funnel, and right into this hole went the boat.

It glided for a long time in a sort of twilight along a subterranean river, which dashed and splashed about him. The air that met him was, at first, chilly and cellar-like; gradually, however, it grew milder and milder, and warmer and warmer.

===Chinese===
冷不防那鱼变得沉重无比、力大无穷，竟拽着钓绳把船也拖走了。

船以骇人的速度飞驶，那湖却仿佛渐渐收缩，渐渐干涸。

湖水朝着鱼游去的方向不可抗拒地倒灌过去——那头是湖底一个漏斗似的窟窿，船便径直灌进了窟窿里。

船沿一条地下河滑行了许久，四下昏昏蒙蒙，河水在他身边奔涌飞溅。迎面而来的空气起初阴冷，如同地窖；渐渐地却越来越温和，越来越暖。

===Original===
The stream now flowed along calmly and quietly, and broadened out continually till it fell into a large lake.

Beyond the borders of this lake, but only half visible in the gloom, stretched swamps and morasses, where he heard sounds as of huge beasts wading and trampling. Serpent like they rose and writhed with a crashing and splashing and snorting amidst the tepid mud and mire.

By the phosphorescent gleams he saw various fishes close to his boat, but all of them lacked eyes.

And he caught glimpses of the outlines of gigantic sea-serpents stretching far away into the darkness. He now understood that it was from down here that they pop up their heads off the coast in the dog days when the sea is warm.

===Chinese===
此刻水流已平缓沉静，一路不断展宽，最后注入一片大湖。

湖岸之外，只在幽暗中隐隐绰绰露出一半的，是绵延的沼泽泥潭，他听得见巨兽跋涉踩踏的声响。它们蛇一般立起身子，在温热的泥淖里翻卷扭动，轰鸣四起，泥浆飞溅，鼻息喷吐。

借着星星点点的磷光，他看见船边游着形形色色的鱼，可全都缺着眼睛。

他还瞥见巨大海蛇的轮廓，远远地伸进黑暗深处。他这才明白：每逢三伏天海水转暖，它们在外海边探出头来，原来正是从这底下钻上去的。

===Original===
The lindworm, with its flat head and duck’s beak, darted after fish, and crept up to the surface of the earth through the slimy ways of mire and marsh.

Through the warm and choking gloom there came, from time to time, a cooling chilling blast from the cold curves and winds of the slimy and slippery greenish lichworm,38 which bores its way through the earth and eats away the coffins that are rotting in the churchyards.

Horrible shapeless monsters, with streaming manes, such as are said to sometimes appear in mountain tarns, writhed and wallowed and seized their prey in the fens and marshes.

And he caught glimpses of all sorts of humanlike creatures, such as fishermen and sailors meet and marvel at on the sea, and landsmen see outside the elfin mounds.

===Chinese===
那林德虫（lindworm）生着扁平的脑袋、鸭子似的喙，箭一般追猎游鱼，又顺着泥沼间的黏滑孔道爬上地面。

温热窒闷的幽暗里，不时掠过一阵清凉砭骨的冷风，来自那尸蠕虫（lichworm）38冰冷盘曲、黏滑溜光的泛绿躯体——它在地下钻行，啃噬着教堂墓园里朽烂的棺木。

还有种种骇人的、不成形的怪物，披着纷披的鬃毛——据说山中小湖里有时会显出它们的踪影——正在沼地里翻滚扭动，攫取猎物。

他还瞥见各式各样人形的生灵，正是渔民水手在海上遇见而啧啧称奇的那些，也是陆居之人在精灵丘（elfin mounds）外望见的那些。

===Original===
And, besides, that there was a soft whizzing and an endless hovering and swarming of beings, whose shapes were nevertheless invisible to the eye of man.

Then the boat glided into miry pulpy water, where her course tended downwards, and where the earth-vault above darkened as it sank lower and lower.

All at once a blinding strip of light shot down from a bright blue slit high, high, above him.

A stuffy vapour stood round about him. The water was as yellow and turbid as that which comes out of steam boilers.

===Chinese===
此外，还有一种轻柔的飒飒声响，无数生灵悬浮不定、成群游动，它们的形骸却是人眼望不见的。

随后船滑进了泥泞浆糊般的水里，航路一路朝下，头顶的地穹也越沉越低，越来越暗。

蓦地，一道刺目的光带从头顶极高极高处一条亮蓝的缝隙里直射而下。

四周弥漫着窒闷的水汽。水色黄浊，就像蒸汽锅炉里放出来的污水一般。

===Original===
And he called to mind the peculiar tepid undrinkable water which bubbles up by the side of artesian wells. It was quite hot. Up there they were boring down to a world of warm watercourses and liquid strata beneath the earth’s crust.

Heat as from an oven rose up from the huge abysses and dizzying clefts, whilst mighty steaming waterfalls roared and shook the ground.

All at once he felt as if his body were breaking loose, freeing itself, and rising in the air. He had a feeling of infinite lightness, of a wondrous capability for floating in higher atmospheres and recovering equilibrium.

And, before he knew how it was, he found himself up on the earth again.

===Chinese===
他想起自流井旁汩汩冒出的那种温吞而不可饮的水。这地方热得很。地面上的人们钻井而下，要凿通的正是地壳底下温水奔流、岩层如液的世界。

烘炉一般的热气从巨大的深渊和令人目眩的裂罅里升腾上来，强大的蒸汽瀑布轰然咆哮，震得大地发颤。

猛然间，他只觉自己的身体正在挣脱、松开，冉冉升上半空。他感到一种无限的轻盈，一种漂浮于高空大气、重获平衡的奇妙能力。

不等他明白过来是怎么回事，他已发觉自己又回到了地面上。

## Jack of Sjöholm and the Gan-Finn / 舍霍尔姆的杰克与甘-芬恩

===Original===
In the days of our forefathers, when there was nothing but wretched boats up in Nordland, and folks must needs buy fair winds by the sackful from the Gan-Finn, it was not safe to tack about in the open sea in wintry weather. In those days a fisherman never grew old. It was mostly womenfolk and children, and the lame and halt, who were buried ashore.
Now there was once a boat’s crew from Thjöttö in Helgeland, which had put to sea, and worked its way right up to the East Lofotens.
But that winter the fish would not bite.
They lay to and waited week after week, till the month was out, and there was nothing for it but to turn home again with their fishing gear and empty boats.

===Chinese===
在我们祖辈的年代，诺尔兰（Nordland）一带还只有些破破烂烂的小船，人们还得成袋成袋地向甘-芬恩（Gan-Finn）买顺风，那时节，寒冬腊月在开阔的海面上抢风行驶可不是闹着玩的事。在那个年代，渔夫从来没有活到老的。能埋进岸上坟地的，大多是妇孺孩童，还有瘸子拐子。

当年，海尔格兰（Helgeland）特约塔（Thjöttö）岛有一船渔人出海，一路捕到了东罗弗敦渔场。

可那年冬天，鱼偏偏不肯咬钩。

他们停船守候，一星期又一星期地干等，等过了一个月，毫无办法，只好带上渔具、驾着空船掉头回家。

===Original===
But Jack of Sjöholm, who was with them, only laughed aloud, and said that, if there were no fish there, fish would certainly be found higher northwards. Surely they hadn’t rowed out all this distance only to eat up all their victuals, said he.
He was quite a young chap, who had never been out fishing before. But there was some sense in what he said for all that, thought the head-fisherman.
And so they set their sails northwards.
On the next fishing-ground they fared no better than before, but they toiled away so long as their food held out.
And now they all insisted on giving it up and turning back.
“If there’s none here, there’s sure to be some still higher up towards the north,” opined Jack; “and if they had gone so far, they might surely go a little further still,” quoth he.

===Chinese===
可同船的舍霍尔姆（Sjöholm）的杰克（Jack）只是放声大笑，说这里没有鱼，往北再上去些，鱼准找得着。他说，总不见得大家划出这么远，就为了把口粮吃个精光吧。

他是个十分年轻的小伙子，从前从没出海捕过鱼。可渔头儿心想，话虽如此，他这话倒也算得上有几分道理。

于是他们扬帆向北。

到了下一处渔场，运气也不比先前好多少，可只要口粮还撑得住，他们就硬着头皮苦干。

到这时，众人一致不肯再干，非要掉头回去不可。

“这里要是没有，再往北上去些准保有，”杰克发表高见；“人都闯到这么远了，”他说，“再往前挪一点点总归行吧。”

===Original===
So they tempted fortune from fishing-ground to fishing-ground, till they had ventured right up to Finmark.15 But there a storm met them, and, try as they might to find shelter under the headlands, they were obliged at last to put out into the open sea again.
There they fared worse than ever. They had a hard time of it. Again and again the prow of the boat went under the heavy rollers, instead of over them, and later on in the day the boat foundered.
There they all sat helplessly on the keel in the midst of the raging sea, and they all complained bitterly against that fellow Jack, who had tempted them on, and led them into destruction. What would now become of their wives and children? They would starve now that they had none to care for them.
When it grew dark, their hands began to stiffen, and they were carried off by the sea one by one.
And Jack heard and saw everything, down to the last shriek and the last clutch; and to the very end they never ceased reproaching him for bringing them into such misery, and bewailing their sad lot.

===Chinese===
就这样，他们从一处渔场到另一处渔场碰运气，一路闯到了芬马克（Finmark）15。可就在那里，一场风暴迎头撞上，他们千方百计想在岬角底下寻个避风处，末了还是只得再冲回开阔的大海。

在那里，他们的境况比先前任何时候都糟。日子苦不堪言。船头一次又一次扎进沉重的巨浪底下，而不是从浪头上掠过去，到那天晚些时候，船沉了。

怒海之中，他们全都束手无策地坐在翻扣的龙骨上，人人切齿埋怨杰克那家伙，是他引着他们一路贪进，把他们领进了毁灭。他们的老婆孩子往后可怎么办？如今没人照管他们，只有挨饿的份了。

天色一暗，众人的手开始发僵，一个接一个地被大海卷走了。

这一切杰克全都听着、看着，直到最后一声惨叫、最后一次抓挠；直到咽气的最后一刻，他们始终没停止责骂他害他们落到这般惨境，哀叹自己命薄。
===Original===
“I must hold on tight now,” said Jack to himself, for he was better even where he was than in the sea.
And so he tightened his knees on the keel, and held on fast till he had no feeling left in either hand or foot.
In the coal-black gusty night he fancied he heard yells from one or other of the remaining boats’ crews.
“They, too, have wives and children,” thought he. “I wonder whether they have also a Jack to lay the blame upon!”
Now while he thus lay there and drifted and drifted, and it seemed to him to be drawing towards dawn, he suddenly felt that the boat was in the grip of a strong shoreward current; and, sure enough, Jack got at last ashore. But whichever way he looked, he saw nothing but black sea and white snow.

===Chinese===
“这会儿我可得死死扒住了，”杰克暗自说，因为就算待在这儿，也总比掉进海里强。

于是他收紧两膝夹住龙骨，死死撑住，直到手脚全都失去了知觉。

在乌黑狂风的夜里，他恍惚听见其余几条残船上人们的呼号。

“他们也有老婆孩子，”他想，“不知他们是不是也有个杰克好拿来怪罪！”

他就这样漂啊漂，漂到天色将明，忽觉船被一股强劲的向岸急流攫住；果然，杰克终于上了岸。可他四下里张望，望见的只有黑黑的海、白白的雪。

===Original===
Now as he stood there, speering and spying about him, he saw, far away, the smoke of a Finn Gamme,16 which stood beneath a cliff, and he managed to scramble right up to it.
The Finn was so old that he could scarcely move. He was sitting in the midst of the warm ashes, and mumbling into a big sack, and neither spoke nor answered. Large yellow humblebees were humming about all over the snow, as if it were Midsummer; and there was only a young lass there to keep the fire alight, and give the old man his food. His grandsons and granddaughters were with the reindeer, far far away on the *Fjeld*.
Here Jack got his clothes well dried, and the rest he so much wanted. The Finn girl, Seimke, couldn’t make too much of him; she fed him with reindeer milk and marrowbones, and he lay down to sleep on silver fox-skins.
Cosy and comfortable it was in the smoke there. But as he thus lay there, ’twixt sleep and wake, it seemed to him as if many odd things were going on round about him.
There stood the Finn in the doorway talking to his reindeer, although they were far away in the mountains. He barred the wolf’s way, and threatened the bear with spells; and then he opened his skin sack, so that the storm howled and piped, and there was a swirl of ashes into the hut. And when all grew quiet again, the air was thick with yellow humblebees, which settled inside his furs, whilst he gabbled and mumbled and wagged his skull-like head.
But Jack had something else to think about besides marvelling at the old Finn. No sooner did the heaviness of slumber quit his eyes than he strolled down to his boat.

===Chinese===
他正站在那里探头探脑地四下打量，远远望见一座芬恩草皮屋（Finn Gamme）16 冒出的炊烟，屋子坐落在一片岩崖底下，他手脚并用，总算一路爬到了那里。

那芬恩老人老得几乎动弹不得。他坐在暖烘烘的灰堆当中，对着一只大皮袋喃喃念咒，不搭话，也不答话。黄色的大蜂在漫地白雪上嗡嗡飞舞，仿佛时值仲夏；屋里只有一个年轻姑娘守着火种，给老人递吃食。他的孙儿孙女们都跟着驯鹿，在遥远遥远的高地（*Fjeld*）上。

在这里，杰克把衣裳烘得干干的，也得了他渴求已久的休憩。芬恩姑娘塞姆克（Seimke）把他疼得没个够；她拿驯鹿奶和髓骨喂他，他躺在银狐皮上睡下了。

烟气里暖融融的，好不舒服。可他这么半睡半醒地躺着，只觉得四下里有种种怪事正在发生。

那芬恩老人站在门口同他的驯鹿说话，尽管驯鹿远在群山之中。他封住狼的路，念咒吓唬熊；随后他打开皮袋，风暴顿时呼啸尖啸，一股灰烬旋进屋来。待一切重归寂静，空中又密密麻麻满是黄蜂，纷纷钻进他的皮裘里栖身，他则咭咭哝哝念念有词，摇着那颗骷髅似的脑袋。

不过，除了惊叹这老芬恩，杰克还有别的心事要想。睡意刚从眼皮上褪尽，他就踱下山去看他的船。

===Original===
There it lay stuck fast on the beach and tilted right over like a trough, while the sea rubbed and rippled against its keel. He drew it far enough ashore to be beyond the reach of the sea-wash.
But the longer he walked around and examined it, the more it seemed to him as if folks built boats rather for the sake of letting the sea in than for the sake of keeping the sea out. The prow was little better than a hog’s snout for burrowing under the water, and the planking by the keel-piece was as flat as the bottom of a chest. Everything, he thought, must be arranged very differently if boats were to be really seaworthy. The prow must be raised one or two planks higher at the very least, and made both sharp and supple, so as to bend before and cut through the waves at the same time, and then a fellow would have a chance of steering a boat smartly.
He thought of this day and night. The only relaxation he had was a chat with the Finn girl of an evening.
He couldn’t help remarking that this Seimke had fallen in love with him. She strolled after him wherever he went, and her eyes always became so mournful when he went down towards the sea; she understood well enough that all his thoughts were bent upon going away.
And the Finn sat and mumbled among the ashes till his fur jacket regularly steamed and smoked.
But Seimke coaxed and wheedled Jack with her brown eyes, and gave him honeyed words as fast as her tongue could wag, till she drew him right into the smoke where the old Finn couldn’t hear them.

===Chinese===
船牢牢搁浅在滩上，像一只水槽似的整个翻扣着，海水摩挲着船底，泛起涟漪。他把船往岸上拖了又拖，直拖到海浪漫不到的地方。

可他围着船转悠端详得越久，心里越发觉得：人们造船，倒像是为了放海水进来，而不是把海水挡在外头。那船头比一头拱水的猪鼻子强不到哪儿去，龙骨旁的船板平得像箱子底。他想，船要真正经得起风浪，一切都得另换个造法。船头至少得再垫高一两块板，还得造得又尖又韧，既能迎浪低头、又能同时破浪而进，这样，人才有机会把一条船使得利利索索。

他白天黑夜想的都是这个。唯一的消遣，是傍晚同芬恩姑娘聊聊天。

他不能不察觉，这塞姆克已经爱上了他。他走到哪儿，她就跟到哪儿；每当他朝海边走去，她的眼睛总要变得那么哀伤；她心里明明白白，他满脑子想的就是离开。

而那芬恩老人坐在灰烬里念念有词，念得皮袄都腾腾地冒热气、冒烟了。

塞姆克却用她那双棕色眼睛百般哄劝杰克，舌尖能转多快，甜言蜜语就递多快，直到把他一直拽进烟气深处，老芬恩听不见的地方。
===Original===
The Gan-Finn turned his head right round.
“My eyes are stupid, and the smoke makes ’em run,” said he; “what has Jack got hold of there?”
“Say it is the white ptarmigan you caught in the snare,” whispered she.
And Jack felt that she was huddling up against him and trembling all over.

===Chinese===
甘-芬恩把脑袋整个转了过来。

“我这双眼拙得很，烟熏得直淌泪，”他说，“杰克搂住的是个什么呀？”

“就说是你套子里捉住的那只白雷鸟，”她悄声道。

杰克觉出她紧紧偎着自己，浑身发抖。

===Original===
Then she told him so softly that he thought it was his own thoughts speaking to him, that the Finn was angry and muttering mischief, and *jöjking*,17 against the boat which Jack wanted to build. If Jack were to complete it, said she, the Gan-Finn would no longer have any sale for his fair-winds in all Nordland. And then she warned him to look to himself and never get between the Finn and the Gan-flies.
Then Jack felt that his boat might be the undoing of him. But the worse things looked, the more he tried to make the best of them.
In the grey dawn, before the Finn was up, he made his way towards the seashore.
But there was something very odd about the snow-hills. They were so many and so long that there was really no end to them, and he kept on trampling in deep and deeper snow and never got to the seashore at all. Never before had he seen the northern lights last so long into the day. They blazed and sparkled, and long tongues of fire licked and hissed after him. He was unable to find either the beach or the boat, nor had he the least idea in the world where he really was.
At last he discovered that he had gone quite astray inland instead of down to the sea. But now, when he turned round, the sea-fog came close up against him, so dense and grey that he could see neither hand nor foot before him.

===Chinese===
接着她轻声细语地告诉他——轻得他只当是自己的心事在开口——说那芬恩动怒了，正对着杰克要造的船咕哝恶咒，约伊克吟咒（*jöjking*）17。她说，杰克若把那船造成功，甘-芬恩在整个诺尔兰的顺风就再也卖不出去了。她还告诫他自己当心，千万莫夹在芬恩和甘蝇（Gan-fly）中间。

杰克这才觉得，这条船也许就是他的祸根。可事情越是不妙，他越要往好里撑。

灰蒙蒙的黎明，趁芬恩还没起身，他便动身往海边去。

可那些雪丘透着说不出的古怪。它们又多又长，绵绵无尽，他一脚深过一脚，在越陷越深的积雪里跋涉，怎么也到不了海边。他从没见过北极光一直亮到白天这么久。极光熊熊闪耀，长长的火舌追在他身后嘶嘶舔动。他既寻不着海滩，也寻不着船，压根儿不知道自己究竟身在何处。

末了他才发现，自己完全走岔了，一头扎进了内陆，而不是下到海边。可这回他一转身，海雾便紧逼上来，又浓又灰，眼前连手和脚都望不见。

===Original===
By the evening he was well-nigh worn out with weariness, and was at his wits’ end what to do.
Night fell, and the snowdrifts increased.
As now he sat him down on a stone and fell a brooding and pondering how he should escape with his life, a pair of snowshoes came gliding so smoothly towards him out of the sea-fog and stood still just in front of his feet.
“As you have found me, you may as well find the way back also,” said he.
So he put them on, and let the snowshoes go their own way over hillside and steep cliff. He let not his own eyes guide him or his own feet carry him, and the swifter he went the denser the snowflakes and the driving sea-spray came up against him, and the blast very nearly blew him off the snowshoes.
Up hill and down dale he went over all the places where he had fared during the daytime, and it sometimes seemed as if he had nothing solid beneath him at all, but was flying in the air.

===Chinese===
到傍晚，他累得几乎散了架，一筹莫展，不知如何是好。

夜幕四合，积雪越堆越厚。

他正往一块石头上坐下，闷头盘算着怎样逃出性命，忽见一双雪鞋从海雾里轻巧之极地滑来，恰恰停在他脚前。

“既然你们寻得着我，”他说，“索性也把回去的路寻出来吧。”

于是他绑上雪鞋，任它们自己走，翻山坡，过陡崖。他不许自己的眼睛引路，也不许自己的双脚使劲；他走得越快，迎面扑来的雪花和飞溅的浪沫便越密，狂风几乎把他整个人从雪鞋上掀下去。

他翻山越谷，白天走过的那些地方一一掠过，有时他恍惚觉得脚下根本没有实地，整个人是在半空里飞。
===Original===
Suddenly the snowshoes stood stock still, and he was standing just outside the entrance of the Gan-Finn’s hut.
There stood Seimke. She was looking for him.
“I sent my snowshoes after thee,” said she, “for I marked that the Finn had bewitched the land so that thou should’st not find the boat. Thy *life* is safe, for he has given thee shelter in his house, but it were not well for thee to see him this evening.”
Then she smuggled him in, so that the Finn did not perceive it in the thick smoke, and she gave him meat and a place to rest upon.
But when he awoke in the night, he heard an odd sound, and there was a buzzing and a singing far away in the air:

> “The Finn the boat can never bind,
 

 The Fly the boatman cannot find,
 

 But round in aimless whirls doth wind.”

===Chinese===
雪鞋蓦地站定，他正立在甘-芬恩小屋的门口。

塞姆克站在那儿。她正出来寻他。

“是我把雪鞋派去追你的，”她说，“因为我看出芬恩对这片土地施了法，叫你寻不着那条船。你的*性命*无碍，因为他已让你在他屋里安了身，可今晚你不宜见他。”

随后她把他偷偷领进屋，浓烟里芬恩毫无察觉；她给了他肉吃，又给了他一处歇息的地方。

可半夜里他醒来，听见一个古怪的声音，半空中远远传来一阵嗡嗡营营的吟唱：

> “芬恩缚不住那条小船，
 

 甘蝇寻不见那驾船人，
 

 只落得没头没脑、团团乱转。”

===Original===
The Finn was sitting among the ashes and *jöjking*, and muttering till the ground quite shook, while Seimke lay with her forehead to the floor and her hands clasped tightly round the back of her neck, praying against him to the Finn God. Then Jack understood that the Gan-Finn was still seeking after him amidst the snowflakes and sea-fog, and that his life was in danger from magic spells.
So he dressed himself before it was light, went out, and came tramping in again all covered with snow, and said he had been after bears in their winter retreats. But never had he been in such a sea-fog before; he had groped about far and wide before he found his way back into the hut again, though he stood just outside it.
The Finn sat there with his skin-wrappings as full of yellow flies as a beehive. He had sent them out searching in every direction, but back they had all come, and were humming and buzzing about him.
When he saw Jack in the doorway, and perceived that the flies had pointed truly, he grew somewhat milder, and laughed till he regularly shook within his skin-wrappings, and mumbled, “The bear we’ll bind fast beneath the scullery-sink, and his eyes I’ve turned all awry,18 so that he can’t see his boat,19 and I’ll stick a sleeping-peg in front of him till springtime.”
But the same day the Finn stood in the doorway, and was busy making magic signs and strange strokes in the air.
Then he sent forth two hideous Gan-flies, which flitted off on their errands, and scorched black patches beneath them in the snow wherever they went. They were to bring pain and sickness to a cottage down in the swamps, and spread abroad the Finn disease, which was to strike down a young bride at Bodö with consumption.

===Chinese===
芬恩正坐在灰烬中约伊克吟咒，喃喃念得地面都震颤起来；塞姆克则前额贴地，双手死死扣住自己的后颈，向芬恩人的神祷告，求神降伏他。杰克这才明白，甘-芬恩还在风雪与海雾之间搜寻他，他的性命正悬在咒术手里。

于是他天不亮就穿好衣裳出去，转脸又浑身是雪地咚咚走了回来，说自己去寻冬眠的熊了。还说从没摊上过这么大的海雾；他明明就站在屋外几步远，却四下摸索了老半天，才找着路回屋。

芬恩坐在那里，皮裘里满是黄蝇，活像一只蜂箱。他已派它们四下搜寻，可它们全数归来，正围着他嗡嗡乱转。

他一见杰克站在门口，知道苍蝇指得没错，火气便消了几分，笑得皮囊里的身子直抖，喃喃道：“这只熊嘛，咱们把他牢牢捆在厨房污水槽底下；他的眼睛我已拨得歪歪斜斜，18 叫他看不见自己的船，19 再在他跟前钉上一枚催眠桩，直钉到开春。”

可就在当天，芬恩又站到门口，忙着朝空中画符，做着一道道怪异的笔画。

随后他放出两只狰狞的甘蝇，让它们各奔差事；它们飞过之处，身下的雪地里都烙出一块块焦黑的斑。它们要去给沼地里的一户人家带去病痛，散播芬恩病，还要击倒博德（Bodö）一个年轻的新娘，叫她染上痨疾。

===Original===
But Jack thought of nothing else night and day but how he could get the better of the Gan-Finn.
The lass Seimke wheedled him and wept and begged him, as he valued his life, not to try to get down to his boat again. At last, however, she saw it was no use﻿—he had made up his mind to be off.
Then she kissed his hands and wept bitterly. At least he must promise to wait till the Gan-Finn had gone right away to Jokmok20 in Sweden.

===Chinese===
可杰克白天黑夜想的只有一件事：怎样才能斗过甘-芬恩。

姑娘塞姆克又是哄又是哭，求他看在性命的份上，千万别再想下海边去他的船。可到头来她见毫无用处——他已铁了心要走。

于是她吻他的手，哭得好不伤心。至少，他得答应等甘-芬恩远赴瑞典的约克莫克（Jokmok）20之后再走。
===Original===
On the day of his departure, the Finn went all round his hut with a torch and took stock.
Far away as they were, there stood the mountain pastures, with the reindeer and the dogs, and the Finn’s people all drew near. The Finn took the tale of the beasts, and bade his grandsons not let the reindeer stray too far while he was away, and could not guard them from wolves and bears. Then he took a sleeping potion and began to dance and turn round and round till his breath quite failed him, and he sank moaning to the ground. His furs were all that remained behind of him. His spirit had gone﻿—gone all the way over to Jokmok.
There the magicians were all sitting together in the dark sea-fog beneath the shelter of the high mountain, and whispering about all manner of secret and hidden things, and blowing spirits into the novices of the black art.
But the Gan-flies, humming and buzzing, went round and round the empty furs of the Gan-Finn like a yellow ring and kept watch.

===Chinese===
动身那天，芬恩举着火把绕屋走了一遭，清点家当。

山中的牧场虽远，此刻却历历在目，驯鹿和狗都在那里，芬恩的族人也一一凑近前来。芬恩清点了牲畜，嘱咐孙儿们在他外出期间别让驯鹿游荡得太远，那时节他无法护着它们防狼防熊。随后他服下一剂安睡的药，开始跳舞，一圈又一圈地旋转，直到气尽力绝，呻吟着瘫倒在地。留下来的只有他那身皮裘。他的魂灵走了——一路走到了约克莫克。

在那边，巫师们正聚坐在高山的庇荫下、黑沉沉的海雾里，交头接耳谈论着种种隐秘玄奥之事，往黑法术的新徒身上吹送魂灵。

而那些甘蝇嗡嗡营营，绕着甘-芬恩空空的皮裘转了一圈又一圈，像一道黄色的环，替他守哨。

===Original===
In the night Jack was awakened by something pulling and tugging at him as if from far away. There was as it were a current of air, and something threatened and called to him from the midst of the snowflakes outside﻿—

> “Until thou canst swim like the duck or the drake,
 

 The egg21 thou’dst be hatching no progress shall make;
 

 The Finn shall ne’er let thee go southwards with sail,
 

 For he’ll screw off the wind and imprison the gale.”

At the end of it the Gan-Finn was standing there, and bending right over him. The skin of his face hung down long and loose, and full of wrinkles, like an old reindeer skin, and there was a dizzying smoke in his eyes. Then Jack began to shiver and stiffen in all his limbs, and he knew that the Finn was bent upon bewitching him.
Then he set his face rigidly against it, so that the magic spells should not get at him; and thus they struggled with one another till the Gan-Finn grew green in the face, and was very near choking.

===Chinese===
夜里，杰克被什么东西扯着拽着惊醒了，那力道仿佛来自极远之处。空气里似有一股暗流，飞雪弥漫的门外，有什么在威吓他、呼唤他——

> “除非你学得鸭子凫水，
 

 你孵的那只蛋21永难有长进；
 

 芬恩绝不容你扬帆南去，
 

 他要拧熄长风，把狂飙囚禁。”

吟到末了，甘-芬恩已立在那里，整个人朝他俯下来。他脸上的皮松松垮垮地垂着，皱纹纵横，像一张老驯鹿皮，眼里腾着一团令人晕眩的烟雾。杰克周身开始发僵发颤，他知道，芬恩是打定主意要对他施咒了。

他绷紧面皮硬顶住，不放咒术沾身；两人就这样暗暗角力，直到甘-芬恩脸都憋绿了，几乎背过气去。

===Original===
After that the sorcerers of Jokmok sent magic shots after Jack, and clouded his wits. He felt so odd; and whenever he was busy with his boat, and had put something to rights in it, something else would immediately go wrong, till at last he felt as if his head were full of pins and needles.
Then deep sorrow fell upon him. Try as he would, he couldn’t put his boat together as he would have it; and it looked very much as if he would never be able to cross the sea again.
But in the summer time Jack and Seimke sat together on the headland in the warm evenings, and the gnats buzzed and the fishes spouted close ashore in the stillness, and the eider-duck swam about.
“If only someone would build me a boat as swift and nimble as a fish, and able to ride upon the billows like a sea-mew!” sighed and lamented Jack, “then I could be off.”

===Chinese===
此后，约克莫克的巫师们又对杰克连发魔弹，搅得他神志昏沉。他浑身不对劲；每当他忙着弄船，刚把这头归置妥帖，那头立刻又出了岔子，到后来，他只觉得满脑袋里扎满了针。

深深的哀愁压上了他。他怎么试，也无法把船拼合成自己想要的样子；照这情形，他怕是永远别想再横渡大海了。

可到了夏天，暖和的傍晚里，杰克和塞姆克并肩坐在岬角上，蚊蚋嗡嗡，鱼儿在静谧中贴岸喷水，绒鸭四处游弋。

“要是有人肯给我造一条船，快得轻巧得像条鱼，能像海鸥一样乘风踏浪，该多好！”杰克长吁短叹，“那我就能走了。”
===Original===
“Would you like me to guide you to Thjöttö?” said a voice up from the seashore.
There stood a fellow in a flat turned-down skin cap, whose face they couldn’t see.
And right outside the boulders there, just where they had seen the eider-duck, lay a long and narrow boat, with high prow and stern; and the tar-boards were mirrored plainly in the clear water below; there was not so much as a single knot in the wood.
“I would be thankful for any such guidance,” said Jack.
When Seimke heard this, she began to cry and take on terribly. She fell upon his neck, and wouldn’t let go, and raved and shrieked. She promised him her snowshoes, which would carry him through everything, and said she would steal for him the bone-stick from the Gan-Finn, so that he might find all the old lucky dollars that ever were buried, and would teach him how to make salmon-catching knots in the fishing lines, and how to entice the reindeer from afar. He should become as rich as the Gan-Finn, if only he wouldn’t forsake her.

===Chinese===
“想不想我引你去特约塔？”海滩下面有个声音说。

那儿立着一个家伙，头戴扁平翻檐的皮帽，脸孔叫人看不清楚。

就在巨石外面，方才绒鸭游弋的地方，泊着一条又长又窄的船，船头船尾高高翘起；涂了焦油的船板清清楚楚倒映在脚下澄澈的水里；那木头上连一个节疤也寻不出。

“这样的引领，我感激不尽，”杰克说。

塞姆克一听这话，登时哭天抢地闹腾起来。她扑上他的脖子，死也不肯撒手，又哭又嚎，语无伦次。她许诺把自己的雪鞋送他，说穿上它能闯过千难万险；还说她要为他从甘-芬恩那里偷来骨杖，好教他寻见历来埋藏地下的所有幸运银元；她要教他在钓线上打捕鲑的结扣，教他从远处诱来驯鹿。只要他不抛下她，他会变得和甘-芬恩一样富有。

===Original===
But Jack had only eyes for the boat down there. Then she sprang up, and tore down her black locks, and bound them round his feet, so that he had to wrench them off before he could get quit of her.
“If I stay here and play with you and the young reindeer, many a poor fellow will have to cling with broken nails to the keel of a boat,”22 said he. “If you like to make it up, give me a kiss and a parting hug, or shall I go without them?”
Then she threw herself into his arms like a young wild cat, and looked straight into his eyes through her tears, and shivered and laughed, and was quite beside herself.
But when she saw she could do nothing with him, she rushed away, and waved her hands above her head in the direction of the Gamme.23
Then Jack understood that she was going to take counsel of the Gan-Finn, and that he had better take refuge in his boat before the way was closed to him. And, in fact, the boat had come so close up to the boulders, that he had only to step down upon the thwarts. The rudder glided into his hand, and aslant behind the mast sat someone at the prow, and hoisted and stretched the sail: but his face Jack could not see.

===Chinese===
可杰克的眼里只有下面那条船。她一跃而起，扯散一头黑发，缠住他的双脚，他只得硬生生扯断发丝，才算挣脱了她。

“我要是留下来，同你和小驯鹿厮闹，往后不知多少可怜人只能用挣裂的指甲，死死抠住船底的龙骨，”22 他说，“你要是不想这么收场，就给我一个吻、一个临别的拥抱；还是让我不带它们就走？”

于是她像只小野猫似的扑进他怀里，泪水后面直直望进他的眼睛，又是发抖又是笑，整个人神魂颠倒。

可待她明白对他无可奈何，便一冲跑开了，朝着草皮屋23 的方向，双手举过头顶挥舞。

杰克登时明白，她这是去向甘-芬恩讨主意，他得趁路还没封死，赶紧上船避祸。果然，那船已紧贴到巨石边上，他只消一步就踏上了座板。船舵自己滑进他手里，桅杆斜后方的船头坐着一个人，正扬帆张索：可那张脸，杰克怎么也看不见。

===Original===
Away they went.
And such a boat for running before the wind Jack had never seen before. The sea stood up round about them like a deep snowdrift, although it was almost calm. But they hadn’t gone very far before a nasty piping began in the air. The birds shrieked and made for land, and the sea rose like a black wall behind them.
It was the Gan-Finn who had opened his wind-sack, and sent a storm after them.
“One needs a full sail in the Finn-cauldron here,” said something from behind the mast.
The fellow who had the boat in hand took such little heed of the weather that he did not so much as take in a single clew.
Then the Gan-Finn sent double knots24 after them.

===Chinese===
船就此启程。

这样一条顺风疾驰的快船，杰克从未见过。四下里的海水耸立起来，像深深的雪堆，虽然海上几乎风平浪静。可没驶出多远，空中便响起一阵刺耳的呼啸。群鸟尖鸣着朝陆地逃窜，他们身后的海像一面黑墙拔地而起。

是甘-芬恩打开了风袋，放一场风暴追他们来了。

“在这口芬恩沸锅里，就得张满帆，”桅杆后面有什么东西说。

掌船的那个家伙全然不把天气放在眼里，连一角帆也懒得收。

于是甘-芬恩又打出双重魔结24，朝他们放了出来。
===Original===
They sped along in a wild dance right over the firth, and the sea whirled up in white columns of foam, reaching to the very clouds.
Unless the boat could fly as quick and quicker than a bird, it was lost.
Then a hideous laugh was heard to larboard﻿—

> “Anfinn Ganfinn gives mouth,
 

 And blows us right south;
 

 There’s a crack25 in the sack,
 

 With three clews we must tack.”

And heeling right over, with three clews in the sail, and the heavy foremost fellow astride on the sheer-strake, with his huge sea-boots dangling in the sea-foam, away they scudded through the blinding spray right into the open sea, amidst the howling and roaring of the wind.

===Chinese===
他们像跳着一场狂舞，飞掠过整条峡湾，海面旋起一根根直抵云霄的白色泡沫巨柱。

除非这船飞得比鸟还快、快上加快，否则只有沉没一途。

突然，左舷传来一声狞笑——

> “安芬恩·甘芬恩张开了口，
 

 径直把我们吹向南方；
 

 风袋25上裂开一道缝，
 

 收起三幅帆角我们才能抢航。”

船身整个倾侧着，帆上收了三个帆角，船头那个沉甸甸的家伙骑在舷缘厚板上，一双巨大的长筒水靴在浪沫里晃荡，他们顶着叫人睁不开眼的飞沫，在风声的呼啸咆哮之中，一路飞驶进开阔的大海。

===Original===
The billowy walls were so vast and heavy that Jack couldn’t even see the light of day across the yards, nor could he exactly make out whether they were going under or over the sea-trough.
The boat shook the sea aside as lightly and easily as if its prow were the slippery fin of a fish, and its planking was as smooth and fine as the shell of a tern’s egg; but, look as he would, Jack couldn’t see where these planks ended; it was just as if there was only half a boat and no more; and at last it seemed to him as if the whole of the front part came off in the sea-foam, and they were scudding along under sail in half a boat.
When night fell, they went through the sea-fire, which glowed like hot embers, and there was a prolonged and hideous howling up in the air to windward.
And cries of distress and howls of mortal agony answered the wind from all the upturned boat keels they sped by, and many hideously pale-looking folks clutched hold of their thwarts. The gleam of the sea-fire cast a blue glare on their faces, and they sat, and gaped, and glared, and yelled at the blast.
Suddenly he awoke, and something cried, “Now thou art at home at Thjöttö, Jack!”
And when he had come to himself a bit, he recognised where he was. He was lying over against the boulders near his boathouse at home. The tide had come so far inland that a border of foam gleamed right up in the potato-field, and he could scarcely keep his feet for the blast. He sat him down in the boathouse, and began scratching and marking out the shape of the Draugboat in the black darkness till sleep overtook him.

===Chinese===
浪墙又高又沉，杰克隔着帆桁都望不见天光，也说不清船究竟是从浪谷底下钻过去，还是从浪脊上面滑过来。

船轻轻巧巧把海水分开，仿佛船头是一面滑溜溜的鱼鳍，船板光滑细洁得像燕鸥的蛋壳；可杰克怎么看，也看不出这些船板的尽头在哪儿；就好像这船只有半条，再无其他；到后来，他只觉整截前半身都化散在浪沫里，他们正扬帆驾着半条船飞驶。

夜幕降临时，他们驶过一片海火，那火像烧红的炭一样灼灼放光，上风处的高空中，一阵悠长而狰狞的嚎叫经久不息。

他们飞掠过一条条翻扣的船底，那上面发出阵阵呼救与垂死的哀嚎，应和着风声；许多惨白得吓人的面孔死死抓住座板。海火的光在他们脸上投下幽幽的青色，他们坐着，张着嘴，瞪着眼，朝着狂风嘶喊。

他蓦地醒来，有什么在喊：“杰克，你到家了，这儿是特约塔！”

待他缓过一点神来，才认出自己身在何处。他正躺在家里船屋旁的巨石边上。潮水内涌得那么远，一道泡沫的白边一直亮到土豆地里，狂风刮得他几乎立不住脚。他坐进船屋，在一片漆黑里勾勾划划，描出德劳格船（Draugboat）的形制，直到睡意将他按倒。

===Original===
When it was light in the morning, his sister came down to him with a meat-basket. She didn’t greet him as if he were a stranger, but behaved as if it were the usual thing for her to come thus every morning. But when he began telling her all about his voyage to Finmark, and the Gan-Finn, and the Draugboat he had come home in at night, he perceived that she only grinned and let him chatter. And all that day he talked about it to his sister and his brothers and his mother, until he arrived at the conclusion that they thought him a little out of his wits. When he mentioned the Draugboat they smiled amongst themselves, and evidently went out of their way to humour him. But they might believe what they liked, if only he could carry out what he wanted to do, and be left to himself in the out-of-the-way old boathouse.
“One should go with the stream,” thought Jack; and if they thought him crazy and out of his wits, he ought to behave so that they might beware of interfering with him, and disturbing him in his work.
So he took a bed of skins with him down to the boathouse, and slept there at night; but in the daytime he perched himself on a pole on the roof, and bellowed out that now he was sailing. Sometimes he rode astraddle on the roof ridge, and dug his sheath-knife deep into the rafters, so that people might think he fancied himself at sea, holding fast on to the keel of a boat.
Whenever folks passed by, he stood in the doorway, and turned up the whites of his eyes so hideously, that everyone who saw him was quite scared. As for the people at home, it was as much as they dared to stick his meat-basket into the boathouse for him. So they sent it to him by his youngest sister, merry little Malfri, who would sit and talk with him, and thought it such fun when he made toys and playthings for her, and talked about the boat which should go like a bird, and sail as no other boat had ever sailed.
If anyone chanced to come upon him unexpectedly, and tried to peep and see what he was about in the boathouse there, he would creep up into the timber-loft and bang and pitch the boards and planks about, so that they didn’t know exactly where to find him, and were glad enough to be off. But one and all made haste to climb over the hill again when they heard him fling himself down at full length and send peal after peal of laughter after them.
So that was how Jack got folks to leave him at peace.

===Chinese===
天亮后，他妹妹提着食盒下来看他。她招呼他，并不像招呼一个生人，倒像她天天早上这样来，本是寻常事。可等他讲起芬马克之行，讲起甘-芬恩，讲起他夜里乘着回家的那条德劳格船，他发觉她只是咧着嘴笑，由他说去。那一整天，他对妹妹、弟弟们和母亲讲个不停，末了得出结论：家里人当他有点疯癫。一提起德劳格船，他们便相视而笑，显然处处顺着他、哄着他。可他们爱信什么信什么，只要他能做成想做的事，让他独自待在这座偏僻的老船屋里就行。

“人就该随大流，”杰克想；既然他们当他疯癫痴傻，他就索性做出个样子，叫他们不敢来搅扰他、妨碍他做活。

于是他抱着一床皮褥搬进船屋，夜里睡在那儿；白天他蹲上屋顶的一根杆子，扯着嗓子吼他正在航行。有时他两腿岔开骑在屋脊上，把鞘刀深深插进椽子，好叫人们以为他恍惚置身海上，正死死扒着一条船的龙骨。

每逢有人路过，他就站在门口，把眼白翻得怕人，见着的人无不胆战。至于家里人，至多只敢把他的食盒塞进船屋了事。于是他们差最小的妹妹、快活的小玛尔弗里（Malfri）给他送饭；她会坐下来陪他说话，他给她做些小玩具小玩意儿，讲起那条要像鸟一样飞驶、要航行得世上无双的船，她只觉得有趣极了。

倘若有人冷不防撞见他，想偷看他在船屋里捣鼓什么，他就爬上堆木料的阁层，把一块块木板船板摔得乒乓乱响，叫人摸不清他究竟藏在哪儿，巴不得赶紧脱身。可只要听见他直挺挺躺倒在地，冲着他们身后一阵接一阵地放声大笑，无论谁都会急急忙忙翻过山丘溜之大吉。

杰克就是这样叫人们不来扰他的清静。
===Original===
He worked best at night when the storm tore and tugged at the stones and birchbark of the turf roof, and the sea-wrack came right up to the boathouse door.
When it piped and whined through the fissured walls, and the fine snowflakes flitted through the cracks, the model of the Draugboat stood plainest before him. The winter days were short, and the wick of the train-oil lamp, which hung over him as he worked, cast deep shadows, so that the darkness came soon and lasted a long way into the morning, when he sought sleep in his bed of skins with a heap of shavings for his pillow.
He spared no pains or trouble. If there was a board which would not run into the right groove with the others, though never so little, he would take out a whole row of them and plane them all round again and again.
Now, one night, just before Christmas, he had finished all but the uppermost planking and the gabs. He was working so hard to finish up that he took no count of time.
The plane was sending the shavings flying their briskest when he came to a dead stop at something black which was moving along the plank.
It was a large and hideous fly which was crawling about and feeling and poking all the planks in the boat. When it reached the lowest keel-board it whirred with its wings and buzzed. Then it rose and swept above it in the air till, all at once, it swerved away into the darkness.

===Chinese===
他夜里干活最出活：风暴撕扯着草皮屋顶的石块和桦树皮，海藻一路冲到船屋门口。

寒风在裂缝纵横的墙壁间呜咽尖啸，细雪从缝隙里飘进来，这时德劳格船的图样在他眼前最是分明。冬日苦短，他干活时悬在头顶的那盏鱼油灯，灯芯投下深深的暗影，于是黑夜来得早，又拖得长，一直拖进清晨，他才枕着一堆刨花，在皮褥上寻他的觉。

他不辞千辛万苦。倘或有一块板与别的板嵌不进同一条槽口，哪怕只差一丝一毫，他也要把整整一排板统统起出来，一遍又一遍地把四面重新刨过。

圣诞前的一夜，他只剩最上一列船板和几处补缝板没有完工。他赶工赶得昏天黑地，全然不记时辰。

刨子正推得刨花纷飞，他却猛地僵住——一样黑黑的东西正沿着船板爬动。

那是一只又大又狰狞的蝇，在船里爬来爬去，把一块块船板摸了个遍、戳了个遍。它爬到最低那块龙骨板跟前，振翅嗡了一声。随后它腾起身，在船板上空来回掠扫，直到猛不丁一个斜飞，遁入黑暗里不见了。

===Original===
Jack’s heart sank within him. Such doubt and anguish came upon him. He knew well enough that no good errand had brought the Gan-fly buzzing over the boat like that.
So he took the train-oil lamp and a wooden club, and began to test the prow and light up the boarding, and thump it well, and go over the planks one by one. And in this way he went over every bit of the boat from stem to stern, both above and below. There was not a nail or a rivet that he really believed in now.
But now neither the shape nor the proportions of the boat pleased him any more. The prow was too big, and the whole cut of the boat all the way down the gunwale had something of a twist and a bend and a swerve about it, so that it looked like the halves of two different boats put together, and the half in front didn’t fit in with the half behind. As he was about to look into the matter still further (and he felt the cold sweat bursting out of the roots of his hair), the train-oil lamp went out and left him in blank darkness.
Then he could contain himself no longer. He lifted his club and burst open the boathouse door, and, snatching up a big cowbell, he began to swing it about him and ring and ring with it through the black night.
“Art chiming for me, Jack?” something asked. There was a sound behind him like the surf sucking at the shore, and a cold blast blew into the boathouse.
There on the keel-stick sat someone in a sloppy grey sea-jacket, and with a print cap drawn down over its ears, so that its skull looked like a low tassel.

===Chinese===
杰克的心往下一沉。疑惑与痛苦一齐涌上心头。他心里明镜似的：甘蝇这样嗡嗡地飞到船上来，准没带着好差事。

于是他提起鱼油灯，抄起一根木棍，开始验船头、照船板，把板子敲得砰砰响，一块块过手。就这样，他把整条船从船头到船尾、从上到下检查了个遍。如今船上没有一颗钉、一颗铆钉是他真正信得过的了。

可这时，船的形制和比例他都不中意了。船头太大，整条船沿舷缘一路下来的线条都带着些扭曲、弯折、偏斜，看着像两条不同的船各取一半拼在一起，前一半和后一半对不上茬。他正要再细究下去（只觉一股冷汗从发根里迸出来），鱼油灯灭了，把他撂在一团漆黑里。

这下他再也按捺不住。他抡起木棍砸开船屋的门，又抓起一只大牛铃，绕着身子抡开，在黑夜里当当地摇个不停。

“杰克，你这是在为我敲钟么？”有什么东西发问。他身后响起一阵浪头吮吸海岸似的声音，一股冷风灌进船屋。

龙骨木上坐着个东西，穿一件湿塌塌的灰色海员褂，一顶印花帽直扣到耳朵根，脑袋看上去活像一绺矮墩墩的帽缨。

===Original===
Jack gave a great start. This was the very being he had been thinking of in his wild rage. Then he took the large baling can and flung it at the Draug.
But right through the Draug it went, and rattled against the wall behind, and back again it came whizzing about Jack’s ears, and if it had struck him he would never have got up again.
The old fellow, however, only blinked his eyes a little savagely.
“Fie!” cried Jack, and spat at the uncanny thing﻿—and back into his face again he got as good as he gave.

===Chinese===
杰克吓了一大跳。这正是他狂怒之中心里想着的那个东西。他抓起那把大舀水桶，朝德劳格（Draug）掷了过去。

那桶径直穿过德劳格，哐当砸在后面的墙上，又嗖地一声折回来，绕着杰克的耳朵呼啸；若是砸中了，他这辈子再别想站起来。

那老家伙却只是把眼皮恶狠狠眨了眨。

“呸！”杰克喊了一声，朝那邪物啐了一口——那口唾沫却原样回到了他自己脸上。
===Original===
“There you have your wet clout back again!” cried a laughing voice.
But the same instant Jack’s eyes were opened and he saw a whole boat-building establishment on the seashore.
And, there, ready and rigged out on the bright water, lay an *Ottring*,26 so long and shapely and shining that his eyes could not feast on it enough.
The old ’un blinked with satisfaction. His eyes became more and more glowing.
“If I could guide you back to Helgeland,” said he, “I could put you in the way of gaining your bread too. But you must pay me a little tax for it. In every seventh boat you build ’tis *I* who must put in the keel-board.”

===Chinese===
“你的湿抹布，这就还给你了！”一个含笑的声音喊道。

可就在这一瞬，杰克的眼睛开了，他看见海滩上整整一座造船场。

那边，明晃晃的水面上，泊着一条装备停当的奥特灵艇（*Ottring*）26，又颀长、又匀称、又亮泽，他的眼睛怎么也看不够。

老家伙心满意足地眨着眼。他的眼睛越来越亮，灼灼放光。

“我既能引你回海尔格兰，”他说，“也能指点你一条糊口的营生。不过你得为此付我一点税。你每造第七条船，都得由*我*来安那块龙骨板。”

===Original===
Jack felt as if he were choking. He felt that the boat was dragging him into the very jaws of an abomination.
“Or do you fancy you’ll worm the trick out of me for nothing?” said the gaping grinning Draug.
Then there was a whirring sound, as if something heavy was hovering about the boathouse, and there was a laugh: “If you want the *seaman’s* boat you must take the *dead man’s* boat along with it. If you knock three times tonight on the keel-piece with the club, you shall have such help in building boats that the like of them will not be found in all Nordland.”
Twice did Jack raise his club that night, and twice he laid it aside again.
But the *Ottring* lay and frisked and sported in the sea before his eyes, just as he had seen it, all bright and new with fresh tar, and with the ropes and fishing gear just put in. He kicked and shook the fine slim boat with his foot just to see how light and high she could rise on the waves above the waterline.
And once, twice, thrice, the club smote against the keel-piece.

===Chinese===
杰克只觉得喉咙哽住，喘不上气。他感到那船正把他往一桩邪祟的血盆大口里拽。

“怎么，你还想白白从我嘴里套出这门手艺？”德劳格咧着大嘴嬉皮笑脸地说。

随后响起一阵扑翼般的声音，仿佛有什么沉重的东西绕着船屋盘旋，还夹着一阵笑：“你要*水手的*船，就得连*死人的*船一并收下。今夜你若用木棍在龙骨木上敲三下，造船时自会得着那样的帮手，造出的船，整个诺尔兰找不出第二条能比的。”

那一夜，杰克两次举起木棍，又两次把它放下。

可那条奥特灵艇就在他眼前的海里翻腾嬉戏，同他方才见到的分毫不差：新涂的焦油锃亮簇新，绳索渔具刚刚归置停当。他用脚又踢又晃那条纤细漂亮的船，只想看看它在水线之上踏浪能蹿得多轻、多高。

于是一下，两下，三下，木棍敲在了龙骨木上。

===Original===
So that was how the first boat was built at Sjöholm.
Thick as birds together stood a countless number of people on the headland in the autumn, watching Jack and his brothers putting out in the new *Ottring*.
It glided through the strong current so that the foam was like a foss all round it.
Now it was gone, and now it ducked up again like a sea-mew, and past skerries and capes it whizzed like a dart.
Out in the fishing grounds the folks rested upon their oars and gaped. Such a boat they had never seen before.
But if in the first year it was an *Ottring*, next year it was a broad heavy Fembøring for winter fishing which made the folks open their eyes.

===Chinese===
舍霍尔姆的第一条船，就是这样造出来的。

秋天，岬角上密密麻麻立满了数不清的人，像落满枝头的鸟，看杰克和他的弟弟们驾着崭新的奥特灵艇出海。

它滑过湍急的潮流，周身的泡沫像一道瀑布绕船奔泻。

忽而没了踪影，忽而又像海鸥一样潜出水面，掠过礁岛与海岬，快得像一支飞镖。

渔场外围，人们停桨张嘴呆看。这样的船，他们平生从未见过。

可如果说头一年是奥特灵艇，第二年叫人们瞪圆眼睛的，则是一条宽阔沉重的芬伯艇（Fembøring），冬日捕鱼用的。
===Original===
And every boat that Jack turned out was lighter to row and swifter to sail than the one before it.
But the largest and finest of all was the last that stood on the stocks on the shore.
This was the *seventh*.
Jack walked to and fro, and thought about it a good deal; but when he came down to see it in the morning, it seemed to him, oddly enough, to have grown in the night and, what is more, was such a wondrous beauty that he was struck dumb with astonishment. There it lay ready at last, and folks were never tired of talking about it.
Now, the Bailiff who ruled over all Helgeland in those days was an unjust man who laid heavy taxes upon the people, taking double weight and tale both of fish and of eiderdown, nor was he less grasping with the tithes and grain dues. Wherever his fellows came they fleeced and flayed. No sooner, then, did the rumour of the new boats reach him than he sent his people out to see what truth was in it, for he himself used to go fishing in the fishing grounds with large crews. When thus his fellows came back and told him what they had seen, the Bailiff was so taken with it that he drove straightway over to Sjöholm, and one fine day down he came swooping on Jack like a hawk. “Neither tithe nor tax hast thou paid for thy livelihood, so now thou shalt be fined as many half-marks of silver as thou hast made boats,” said he.
Ever louder and fiercer grew his rage. Jack should be put in chains and irons and be transported northwards to the fortress of Skraar, and be kept so close that he should never see sun or moon more.

===Chinese===
杰克造出的每一条船，都比前一条更轻于划、更疾于驶。

而最大最漂亮的一条，是滩上船台上立着的最后一条。

这是*第七*条。

杰克踱来踱去，把这事翻来覆去想了许久；可早晨他下来看船时，说来奇怪，它仿佛在夜里又长了长个头，而且出落得那样奇美，惊得他哑口无言。它终于在那儿备置停当，人们对它百谈不厌。

那时节，统辖整个海尔格兰的邑宰（Bailiff）是个不义之人，向百姓横征重税：收鱼收绒鸭绒，秤重计数都要加倍；收什一税和谷物杂捐，贪心也不稍减。他的爪牙所到之处，敲骨吸髓。新船的名声一传进他耳朵，他立刻派人出去查探虚实——因为他自己平日常带着大队人马去渔场捕鱼。待爪牙们回来禀报所见，邑宰艳羡得坐不住了，径直驱程赶往舍霍尔姆，一个晴和的日子，他像鹰隼扑食一般朝杰克当头扑下。“你靠这营生，什一税课税分文未缴，如今就罚你——造了几条船，缴几个半马克的银子，”他说。

他的怒火越烧越炽，越烧越凶。杰克要被钉上镣铐锁链，押解北上，关进斯克劳要塞（Skraar），严加囚禁，从此再不见日月。

===Original===
But when the Bailiff had rowed round the Fembøring, and feasted his eyes upon it, and seen how smart and shapely it was, he agreed at last to let Mercy go before Justice, and was content to take the Fembøring in lieu of a fine.
Then Jack took off his cap and said that if there was one man more than another to whom he would like to give the boat, it was his honour the Bailiff.
So off the magistrate sailed with it.
Jack’s mother and sister and brothers cried bitterly at the loss of the beautiful Fembøring; but Jack stood on the roof of the boathouse and laughed fit to split.
And towards autumn the news spread that the Bailiff with his eight men had gone down with the Fembøring in the West-fjord.
But in those days there was quite a changing about of boats all over Nordland, and Jack was unable to build a tenth part of the boats required of him. Folks from near and far hung about the walls of his boathouse, and it was quite a favour on his part to take orders, and agree to carry them out. A whole score of boats soon stood beneath the penthouse on the strand.

===Chinese===
可邑宰绕着芬伯艇划了一遭，饱看一番，见它那样轻灵匀称，末了总算允准让慈悲先行于公义，肯收下这条芬伯艇抵了罚金。

杰克于是脱帽道：天底下若有一个人是他最乐意奉送这条船的，那必是邑宰大人阁下。

这位长官便驾着船扬长而去。

痛失漂亮的芬伯艇，杰克的母亲、妹妹和弟弟们哭得好不伤心；杰克却站在船屋顶上，笑得差点背过气去。

到了秋天，消息传开：邑宰连同他的八名随从，连人带芬伯艇沉在了西峡湾（West-fjord）。

那时节，整个诺尔兰的船只正经历一场大换新，登门求船的，杰克十成里造不上一成。远近的人们守在船屋墙外，他肯接下订单、应承动工，便是天大的情面。不多久，滩头披棚下便立起了足足二十条船。

===Original===
He no longer troubled his head about every *seventh* boat, or cared to know which it was or what befell it. If a boat foundered now and then, so many the more got off and did well, so that, on the whole, he made a very good thing indeed out of it. Besides, surely folks could pick and choose their own boats, and take which they liked best.
But Jack got so great and mighty that it was not advisable for anyone to thwart him, or interfere where he ruled and reigned.
Whole rows of silver dollars stood in the barrels in the loft, and his boat-building establishment stretched over all the islands of Sjöholm.
One Sunday his brothers and merry little Malfri had gone to church in the Fembøring. When evening came, and they hadn’t come home, the boatman came in and said that someone had better sail out and look after them, as a gale was blowing up.
Jack was sitting with a plumb-line in his hand, taking the measurements of a new boat, which was to be bigger and statelier than any of the others, so that it was not well to disturb him.
“Do you fancy they’re gone out in a rotten old tub, then?” bellowed he. And the boatman was driven out as quickly as he had come.

===Chinese===
他再也不为每条*第七*的船操心了，也不管它是哪一条、落了什么下场。间或沉上一条，安然脱险、顺风顺水的便多出许多，总的说来，他这买卖实在做得不赖。再说，船本是人家自己挑自己选的，爱要哪条要哪条。

杰克就这样势大气粗，谁也犯不上触他的霉头，或到他称雄称霸的地界里插手。

一桶桶银元在阁楼里码成一排排，他的造船场铺遍了舍霍尔姆诸岛。

一个礼拜天，他的弟弟们和快活的小玛尔弗里驾着芬伯艇上教堂去了。到了傍晚还不见人影，船工进来说，起风了，眼看要变天，最好有人驾船出去照应照应。

杰克手里正捏着铅垂线，给一条新船量尺寸——这条船要比先前所有的都更大、更气派——这种时候不便搅扰他。

“怎么，你当他们乘的是条朽木破桶么？”他吼道。船工进来多快，被轰出去就有多快。
===Original===
But at night Jack lay awake and listened. The wind whined outside and shook the walls, and there were cries from the sea far away. And just then there came a knocking at the door, and someone called him by name.
“Go back whence you came,” cried he, and nestled more snugly in his bed.
Shortly afterwards there came the fumbling and the scratching of tiny fingers at the door.
“Can’t you leave me at peace o’ nights?” he bawled, “or must I build me another bedroom?”

===Chinese===
可到了夜里，杰克醒着躺在床上听。风在屋外呜咽，摇得四壁直响，远处海上有人声哭喊。正在这时，门上响起敲击声，有什么在唤他的名字。

“从哪儿来，回哪儿去，”他喊了一声，往被窝里缩得更舒服了些。

不多时，门上又传来细小手指摸索抓挠的动静。

“你们夜里也不能让我安生么？”他大吼，“还是逼着我再给自己盖一间卧室？”

===Original===
But the knocking and the fumbling for the latch outside continued, and there was a sweeping sound at the door, as of someone who could not open it. And there was a stretching of hands towards the latch ever higher and higher.
But Jack only lay there and laughed. “The Fembørings that are built at Sjöholm don’t go down before the first blast that blows,” mocked he.
Then the latch chopped and hopped till the door flew wide open, and in the doorway stood pretty Malfri and her mother and brothers. The sea-fire shone about them, and they were dripping with water.
Their faces were pale and blue, and pinched about the corners of the mouth, as if they had just gone through their death agony. Malfri had one stiff arm round her mother’s neck; it was all torn and bleeding, just as when she had gripped her for the last time. She railed and lamented, and begged back her young life from him.
So now he knew what had befallen them.
Out into the dark night and the darker weather he went straightway to search for them, with as many boats and folk as he could get together. They sailed and searched in every direction, and it was in vain.

===Chinese===
可门外的敲击和摸索门闩还在继续，门上还有一种扫掠般的声音，像一个开不了门的人在那里蹭来蹭去。还有一只只手朝门闩够上去，越够越高。

杰克却只是躺着笑。“舍霍尔姆造的芬伯艇，可不是风头一阵就沉的，”他嘲弄道。

话音未落，门闩劈啪乱蹦，房门砰然大开，门口立着漂亮的玛尔弗里，还有她的母亲和弟弟们。海火的光环着他们，人人身上滴着水。

他们的脸青白灰败，嘴角扭曲紧缩，像刚挣过一场临死的苦痛。玛尔弗里一条僵直的胳膊还搂着母亲的脖子；那条胳膊撕裂了，淌着血，正是她最后一刻死死抓住母亲时的模样。她又是怨骂又是哀哭，向他讨还自己年轻的生命。

这下他知道他们遭了什么祸了。

他当即出动，闯进黑夜和比夜更黑的风天里去寻他们，纠集了能纠集的所有船只人手。他们扬帆四下搜索，一无所获。

===Original===
But towards day the Fembøring came drifting homewards bottom upwards, and with a large hole in the keel-board.
Then he knew who had done the deed.
But since the night when the whole of Jack’s family went down, things were very different at Sjöholm.
In the daytime, so long as the hammering and the banging and the planing and the clinching rang about his ears, things went along swimmingly, and the frames of boat after boat rose thick as sea fowl on an *Æggevær*.27

===Chinese===
可天将亮时，那条芬伯艇底朝天漂了回来，龙骨板上破着一个大洞。

他这才明白是谁做下的事。

可自打杰克全家沉海的那一夜起，舍霍尔姆的一切都变了样。

白天，只要锤声、敲击声、刨声、钉板声还在他耳边响着，一切便顺顺当当，一条又一条船的肋骨次第立起，稠密得像埃格沃尔（*Æggevær*）27 渔村上的海鸟。
===Original===
But no sooner was it quiet of an evening than he had company. His mother bustled and banged about the house, and opened and shut drawers and cupboards, and the stairs creaked with the heavy tread of his brothers going up to their bedrooms.
At night no sleep visited his eyes, and sure enough pretty Malfri came to his door and sighed and groaned.
Then he would lie awake there and think, and reckon up how many boats with false keel-boards he might have sent to sea. And the longer he reckoned the more draug-boats he made of it.
Then he would plump out of bed and creep through the dark night down to the boathouse. There he held a light beneath the boats, and banged and tested all the keel-boards with a club to see if he couldn’t hit upon the *seventh*. But he neither heard nor felt a single board give way. One was just like another. They were all hard and supple, and the wood, when he scraped off the tar, was white and fresh.
One night he was so tormented by an uneasiness about the new *Sekstring*,28 which lay down by the bridge ready to set off next morning, that he had no peace till he went down and tested its keel-board with his club.
But while he sat in the boat, and was bending over the thwart with a light, there was a gulping sound out at sea, and then came such a vile stench of rottenness. The same instant he heard a wading sound, as of many people coming ashore, and then up over the headland he saw a boat’s crew coming along.

===Chinese===
可一到晚上静下来，他便有了伴。母亲在屋里忙忙碌碌，摔摔打打，抽屉柜门开了又关；楼梯在他弟弟们沉重的脚步下吱呀呻吟，他们上楼回卧房去。

夜里他合不上眼，果然，漂亮的玛尔弗里来到他门口，一声接一声地叹息呻吟。

他便这样睁眼躺着盘算，数着自己究竟送了多少条安着假龙骨板的船下海。越数，那数目里的德劳格船就越多。

于是他会一骨碌跳下床，摸黑潜到船屋去。他在船底举灯照着，用木棍把一条条龙骨板敲了个遍，看能不能撞出那条*第七*来。可没有一块板听出或摸出半点松动。条条都一模一样，又坚实又柔韧，刮开焦油，木头白生生的，透着新鲜。

一夜，他心里悬着那条新塞克斯特灵艇（*Sekstring*）28，受着煎熬不得安宁——船就泊在桥边，明早就要启程——他按捺不住，下去用木棍敲验它的龙骨板。

可他正坐在船里，举灯俯身对着座板，海上忽然响起一阵吞咽似的咕咚声，接着涌来一股腐臭，臭得令人作呕。同一瞬间，他听见涉水的声响，像许多人正走上岸来；随后他望见一船人影翻过海岬，迤逦而来。

===Original===
They were all crooked-looking creatures, and they all leaned right forward and stretched out their arms before them. Whatever came in their way, both stone and stour,29 they went right through it, and there was neither sound nor shriek.
Behind them came another boat’s crew, big and little, grown men and little children, rattling and creaking.
And crew after crew came ashore and took the path leading to the headland.
When the moon peeped forth Jack could see right into their skeletons. Their faces glared, and their mouths gaped open with glistening teeth, as if they had been swallowing water. They came in heaps and shoals, one after the other: the place quite swarmed with them.
Then Jack perceived that here were all they whom he had tried to count and reckon up as he lay in bed, and a fit of fury came upon him.
He rose in the boat and spanked his leather breeches behind and cried: “You would have been even more than you are already if Jack hadn’t built his boats!”

===Chinese===
他们个个佝偻扭曲，个个前倾着身子，双臂直直前伸。路上无论撞着什么，石头也罢、尘土 29 也罢，都径直穿了过去，没有一点声响，没有一声尖叫。

他们身后又来了一船人，大大小小，成年的汉子和幼小的孩童，浑身咔咔作响。

一船又一船的人上了岸，踏上海岬那条小路。

月亮探出头来时，杰克能一直望进他们的骸骨里。一张张脸惨白刺目，一张张嘴大张着，牙齿幽幽发亮，仿佛还在往肚里咽水。他们成群结队，一拨接一拨涌来：这地方简直让他们挤满了。

杰克认出来了：这正是他躺在床上想点数清算的那些人。一阵狂怒攫住了他。

他从船里站起身，朝自己皮裤后裆拍了一巴掌，喊道：“要不是杰克造船，你们这帮家伙还会比现在更多哩！”

===Original===
But now like an icy whizzing blast they all came down upon him, staring at him with their hollow eyes.
They gnashed their teeth, and each one of them sighed and groaned for his lost life.
Then Jack, in his horror, put out from Sjöholm.
But the sail slackened, and he glided into dead water.30 There, in the midst of the still water, was a floating mass of rotten swollen planks. All of them had once been shaped and fashioned together, but were now burst and sprung, and slime and green mould and filth and nastiness hung about them.

===Chinese===
可这一下，他们像一阵冰冷呼啸的狂风，齐压压朝他扑来，一双双空洞的眼睛死盯着他。

他们咬牙切齿，一个个为自己失落的生命叹息呻吟。

杰克惊怖万分，驾船逃离了舍霍尔姆。

可帆渐渐软了下去，他滑进了死水（dead water）30。那里，凝滞的水面中央，漂浮着一堆朽烂发胀的船板。它们当初都曾被拼在一起凿造成形，如今却崩裂开张，黏泥、绿霉、污垢、秽物缠挂其上。
===Original===
Dead hands grabbed at the corners of them with their white knuckles and couldn’t grip fast. They stretched themselves across the water and sank again.
Then Jack let out all his clews and sailed and sailed and tacked according as the wind blew.
He glared back at the rubbish behind him to see if those *things* were after him. Down in the sea all the dead hands were writhing, and tried to strike him with gaffs astern.
Then there came a gust of wind whining and howling, and the boat drove along betwixt white seething rollers.
The weather darkened, thick snowflakes filled the air, and the rubbish around him grew greener.

===Chinese===
一只只死人的手，白森森的指节抠着船板边角，却抠捏不住。他们朝水面那头伸展开去，又沉了下去。

杰克于是放开所有帆角，扬帆再扬帆，随风抢风行驶。

他回头瞪着身后那堆破烂，看那些*东西*追上来没有。海水深处，所有死人的手都在扭动，想从船尾用搭钩钩他。

随后一阵狂风呜呜呼啸而来，船在白浪翻腾的巨涌之间疾驰。

天色昏暗下来，鹅毛大雪漫天飞卷，他四周那堆破烂泛得越来越绿。

===Original===
In the daytime he took the cormorants far away in the grey mist for his landmarks, and at night they screeched about his ears.
And the birds flitted and flitted continually, but Jack sat still and looked out upon the hideous cormorants.
At last the sea-fog lifted a little, and the air began to be alive with bright, black, buzzing flies. The sun burned, and far away inland the snowy plains blazed in its light.
He recognised very well the headland and shore where he was now able to lay to. The smoke came from the Gamme up on the snow-hill there. In the doorway sat the Gan-Finn. He was lifting his pointed cap up and down, up and down, by means of a thread of sinew, which went right through him, so that his skin creaked.
And up there also sure enough was Seimke.
She looked old and angular as she bent over the reindeer-skin that she was spreading out in the sunny weather. But she peeped beneath her arm as quick and nimble as a cat with kittens, and the sun shone upon her, and lit up her face and pitch-black hair.

===Chinese===
白天，他把远远浮在灰雾里的鸬鹚当作航标；夜里，它们就在他耳边尖声哀叫。

鸬鹚们不住地飞掠、飞掠，杰克却端坐不动，望着那一片狰狞的鸬鹚。

终于，海雾微微掀起一角，空气里开始活跃起一只只油亮乌黑、嗡嗡营营的蝇。太阳灼晒着，遥远的内陆，雪原在日光下灿然生辉。

他一眼认出了这处海岬和这片海岸——如今他能在此落锚驻船了。炊烟来自那边雪丘上的草皮屋。甘-芬恩坐在门口。他正借着一根穿过全身的筋腱细线，把那顶尖帽一提一落、一提一落，牵得他浑身的皮咯咯作响。

上面果然还有塞姆克。

她俯身摊晒驯鹿皮，在晴光里显得苍老而嶙峋。可她从胳膊底下偷眼张望时，还像一只护崽的母猫般机敏利落；阳光照在她身上，照亮了她的脸和那头乌黑的头发。

===Original===
She leaped up so briskly, and shaded her eyes with her hand, and looked down at him. Her dog barked, but she quieted it so that the Gan-Finn should mark nothing.
Then a strange longing came over him, and he put ashore.
He stood beside her, and she threw her arms over her head, and laughed and shook and nestled close up to him, and cried and pleaded, and didn’t know what to do with herself, and ducked down upon his bosom, and threw herself on his neck, and kissed and fondled him, and wouldn’t let him go.
But the Gan-Finn had noticed that there was something amiss, and sat all the time in his furs, and mumbled and muttered to the Gan-flies, so that Jack dare not get between him and the doorway.
The Finn was angry.
Since there had been such a changing about of boats over all Nordland, and there was no more sale for his fair winds, he was quite ruined, he complained. He was now so poor that he would very soon have to go about and beg his bread. And of all his reindeer he had only a single doe left, who went about there by the house.

===Chinese===
她轻捷地一跃而起，抬手遮着眼睛，朝下面望他。她的狗叫起来，她把它喝住，免得甘-芬恩察觉什么。

一种奇异的渴望攫住了他，他把船靠了岸。

他站到她身旁，她双臂甩过头顶，又是笑又是颤，紧紧偎进他怀里，又哭又求，整个人不知如何是好，一头埋进他胸口，又扑上他的脖颈，亲他抚他，怎么也不肯放他走。

可甘-芬恩已察觉出几分不对，一直坐在他的皮裘里，对着甘蝇们咕哝不休，杰克不敢夹在他和门槛中间。

芬恩动怒了。

他抱怨说，自从整个诺尔兰的船只这般大换新，他的顺风再无人来买，他就彻底败落了。如今他穷得很快就要挨家讨饭了。满群驯鹿，只剩下一头母鹿，还在屋子近旁转来转去。
===Original===
Then Seimke crept behind Jack, and whispered to him to bid for this doe. Then she put the reindeer-skin around her, and stood inside the Gamme door in the smoke, so that the Gan-Finn only saw the grey skin, and fancied it was the reindeer they were bringing in.
Then Jack laid his hand upon Seimke’s neck, and began to bid.
The pointed cap ducked and nodded, and the Finn spat in the warm air; but sell his reindeer he would not.
Jack raised his price.
But the Finn heaved up the ashes all about him, and threatened and shrieked. The flies came as thick as snowflakes; the Finn’s furry wrappings were alive with them.
Jack bid and bid till it reached a whole bushel load of silver, and the Finn was ready to jump out of his skins.

===Chinese===
塞姆克便悄悄绕到杰克身后，怂恿他出价买这头母鹿。随后她把那张驯鹿皮裹在身上，站进草皮屋门口的烟气里，甘-芬恩便只看见一团灰皮子，只当他们牵进来的是那头鹿。

杰克便把手搭在塞姆克的脖颈上，开始出价。

尖顶帽一颔一点，芬恩朝暖烘烘的空气里啐着唾沫；可他的驯鹿，他死活不肯卖。

杰克抬高了价钱。

芬恩却把周身的灰烬统统掀腾起来，又叫又吓。蝇群密得像雪片一样压来；他的皮裘里里外外爬满了蝇，活了一般。

杰克一价接一价地加，加到整整一蒲式耳银子，芬恩气得险些从皮裘里蹦出来。

===Original===
Then he stuck his head under his furs again, and mumbled and *jöjked* till the amount rose to seven bushels of silver.
Then the Gan-Finn laughed till he nearly split. He thought the reindeer would cost the purchaser a pretty penny.
But Jack lifted Seimke up, and sprang down with her to his boat, and held the reindeer-skin behind him, against the Gan-Finn.
And they put off from land, and went to sea.

===Chinese===
随后他又把脑袋缩回皮裘底下，又咕哝又约伊克吟咒，把价钱一直咒到了七蒲式耳银子。

甘-芬恩这才笑得几乎绽裂。他想，这头驯鹿可要教买主破费一笔大钱了。

可杰克一把抱起塞姆克，带着她一跃跳上自己的船，把那张驯鹿皮举在身后，正对着甘-芬恩。

他们离了岸，驶向大海。

===Original===
Seimke was so happy, and smote her hands together, and took her turn at the oars.
The northern light shot out like a comb, all greeny-red and fiery, and licked and played upon her face. She talked to it, and fought it with her hands, and her eyes sparkled. She used both tongue and mouth and rapid gestures as she exchanged words with it.
Then it grew dark, and she lay on his bosom, so that he could feel her warm breath. Her black hair lay right over him, and she was as soft and warm to the touch as a ptarmigan when it is frightened and its blood throbs.
Jack put the reindeer-skin over Seimke, and the boat rocked them to and fro on the heavy sea as if it were a cradle.
They sailed on and on till nightfall; they sailed on and on till they saw neither headland nor island nor seabird in the outer skerries more.

===Chinese===
塞姆克快活极了，拍着双手，轮换着也操起桨来。

北极光像一把梳子般迸射开来，绿里透红，烈焰腾腾，在她的脸上舔动嬉戏。她同它说话，挥着双手同它打斗，眼睛闪闪发亮。她同它你来我往，口舌并用，手势翻飞。

后来天黑了，她伏在他胸膛上，他感觉得到她温暖的呼吸。她的黑发铺撒在他身上，她摸上去又软又暖，像一只受惊的雷鸟，血脉突突直跳。

杰克把驯鹿皮盖在塞姆克身上，船在沉重的海面上摇着他们晃来晃去，像一只摇篮。

他们驶啊驶，一直驶到夜幕四合；驶啊驶，直到外海礁岛间再也望不见海岬、岛屿和海鸟。

## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===

1. *Evje* signifies a stream with a slow current. ↩︎

2. Norwegian peasants bear the same name as their estates. As there are no nobles in Norway, the farmers or owners of estates occupy a much higher social position than in Germany. ↩︎

3. The *Skydo-Bauern* along the principal roads supply travellers with beds and food. ↩︎

4. A district in northern Norway. ↩︎

1. *Evje*（埃维）意为一条水流缓慢的小河。 ↩︎

2. 挪威农民与自己的田庄同名。挪威没有贵族，因此农民亦即田庄主人所占的社会地位，远比德国的农民为高。 ↩︎

3. 大路沿线的斯基多农户（*Skydo-Bauern*）为旅客提供床铺和饮食。 ↩︎

4. 北挪威的一个地区。 ↩︎

===Original===

5. A boat with three oars on each side. ↩︎

6. A long pole, with a hooked iron spike at the end of it, for spearing *Kvejte* or hallibut with. ↩︎

7. A large boat with five oars on each side, used for winter fishing in northern Norway. ↩︎

8. The chief port in those parts. ↩︎

5. 每侧各配三支桨的船。 ↩︎

6. 一根长杆，末端装有带倒钩的铁尖，用来叉捕 *Kvejte*（大比目鱼）。 ↩︎

7. 每侧各配五支桨的大船，用于北挪威的冬季捕捞。 ↩︎

===Original===

9. *Hin Karen* = “the devil.” “Karen” is the Danish “Karl.” ↩︎

10. The *Klör*, or clews, were rings in the corner of the sail to fasten it down by in a strong wind. *Setja ei Klo* = “take in the sail a clew.” *Setja tvo*, or *tri Klör* = “take it in two or three clews,” I.e., diminish it still further as the wind grew stronger. ↩︎

11. A demon peculiar to the north Norwegian coast. It rides the seas in a half-boat. Compare Icelandic *draugr*. ↩︎

12. See note 6. ↩︎

9. *Hin Karen*＝「魔鬼」。「Karen」即丹麦语的「Karl」。 ↩︎

10. *Klör* 即帆角环，是帆角上的环扣，风大时用来把帆收紧扣牢。*Setja ei Klo*＝「收一个帆角」。*Setja tvo* 或 *tri Klör*＝「收两三个帆角」，也就是说，风势愈猛，帆面便愈收愈小。 ↩︎

11. 北挪威海岸特有的一种恶魔，驾着半条船在海上游弋。参较冰岛语 *draugr*。 ↩︎

15. The northernmost province of Norway, right within the Arctic circle. ↩︎

16. The huts peculiar to the Norwegian Finns. ↩︎

37. *Hulder, huldre*，泛指一切精灵妖属的名目。参较冰岛语 *Hulda*，意为遮藏、掩覆；它暗示精灵一族的隐形无踪。 ↩︎

38. *Ligorm*，食尸之蛇。英语里既有 Lichfield（利奇菲尔德）和 lichgate（墓门），自然也可以有 lichworm（尸蠕虫）。 ↩︎

## Finn-Blood / 芬恩之血

===Original===
In Svartfjord, north of Senje, dwelt a lad called Eilert. His neighbours were seafaring Finns, and among their children was a pale little girl, remarkable for her long black hair and her large eyes. They dwelt behind the crag on the other side of the promontory, and fished for a livelihood, as also did Eilert’s parents; wherefore there was no particular goodwill between the families, for the nearest fishing ground was but a small one, and each would have liked to have rowed there alone.

Nevertheless, though his parents didn’t like it at all, and even forbade it, Eilert used to sneak regularly down to the Finns. There they had always strange tales to tell, and he heard wondrous things about the recesses of the mountains, where the original home of the Finns was, and where, in the olden time, dwelt the Finn Kings, who were masters among the magicians. There, too, he heard tell of all that was beneath the sea, where the Mermen and the Draugs hold sway. The latter are gloomy evil powers, and many a time his blood stood still in his veins as he sat and listened. They told him that the Draug usually showed himself on the strand in the moonlight on those spots which were covered with sea-wrack; that he had a bunch of seaweed instead of a head, but shaped so peculiarly that whoever came across him absolutely couldn’t help gazing into his pale and horrible face. They themselves had seen him many a time, and once they had driven him, thwart by thwart, out of the boat where he had sat one morning, and turned the oars upside down. When Eilert hastened homewards in the darkness round the headland, along the strand, over heaps of seaweed, he dare scarcely look around him, and many a time the sweat absolutely streamed from his forehead.

In proportion as hostility increased among the old people, they had a good deal of fault to find with one another, and Eilert heard no end of evil things spoken about the Finns at home. Now it was this, and now it was that. They didn’t even row like honest folk, for, after the Finnish fashion, they took high and swift strokes, as if they were womenkind, and they all talked together, and made a noise while they rowed, instead of being “silent in the boat.” But what impressed Eilert most of all was the fact that, in the Finnwoman’s family, they practised sorcery and idolatry, or so folks said. He also heard tell of something beyond all question, and that was the shame of having Finn blood in one’s veins, which also was the reason why the Finns were not as good as other honest folk, so that the magistrates gave them their own distinct burial-ground in the churchyard, and their own separate “Finn-pens” in church. Eilert had seen this with his own eyes in the church at Berg.

===Chinese===
在塞尼亚（Senje）以北的斯瓦特峡湾（Svartfjord），住着一个名叫埃勒特（Eilert）的小伙子。他的邻居是以海为生的芬恩人，他们的孩子当中有一个面色苍白的小姑娘，一头长长的黑发、一双大大的眼睛，格外惹眼。他们住在海岬那面岩嶂的后头，靠打鱼过活，埃勒特的父母也一样；因此两家之间谈不上什么情分，因为最近的渔场不过是巴掌大的一块，谁都恨不得独自一个去下桨。

然而，尽管父母一百个不乐意，甚至明令禁止，埃勒特还是照旧一趟趟偷偷溜到芬恩人那里去。他们那儿总有离奇的故事可讲，他听来了大山腹地种种奇妙的事情——那里是芬恩人古老的家园，古时候住着芬恩诸王（Finn Kings），是术士行中的魁首。在那里，他还听到海底的一切，那是人鱼（Merman）与德劳格（Draug）们称雄的领域。后者是阴郁的邪祟，他坐在一旁听着，好几次血都在血管里凝住。他们告诉他，德劳格常在月夜里现身于滩头铺满海藻的地方；他拿一丛海藻权当脑袋，形状却又偏偏生得那样古怪，撞上他的人便由不得自己，非要把目光钉在他那张苍白可怕的脸膛上不可。他们自己就见识过他许多回，有一回还顺着一条条座板把他撵出了船外——一天早晨他就坐在那船里——又把船桨一根根倒放过来。埃勒特摸黑绕过海岬、沿着滩头、跨过一堆堆海藻往家赶的时候，几乎不敢朝四下里张望，好几次汗珠子简直是顺着额头淌成了河。

老一辈人之间的仇怨越结越深，彼此挑出的毛病也越来越多，埃勒特在家里听到数落芬恩人的坏话，就没完没了。一会儿是这个，一会儿又是那个。他们连划船都不像正经人家的做派：照芬兰人的路数，桨抬得又高又急，活脱脱像娘儿们；划起船来还个个抢着讲话，闹闹哄哄，全不懂得“船中宜静”。可给埃勒特印象最深的，还是据说那芬恩女人家里行巫术、拜邪神——至少人家都这么讲。他还听说了一件板上钉钉的事：血管里流着芬恩之血，便是耻辱。这也正是芬恩人不如别家正经人的缘故，所以官府在教堂墓地里给他们单独划了一块坟地，教堂里还给他们专设“芬恩栏（Finn-pens）”。这埃勒特在贝尔格（Berg）教堂里是亲眼见过的。

===Original===
All this made him very angry, for he could not help liking the Finn folks down yonder, and especially little Zilla. They two were always together: she knew such a lot about the Merman. Henceforth his conscience always plagued him when he played with her; and whenever she stared at him with her large black eyes while she told him tales, he used to begin to feel a little bit afraid, for at such times he reflected that she and her people belonged to the Damned, and that was why they knew so much about such things. But, on the other hand, the thought of it made him so bitterly angry, especially on her account. She, too, was frequently taken aback by his odd behaviour towards her, which she couldn’t understand at all; and then, as was her wont, she would begin laughing at and teasing him by making him run after her, while she went and hid herself.

One day he found her sitting on a boulder by the seashore. She had in her lap an eider duck which had been shot, and could only have died quite recently, for it was still warm, and she wept bitterly over it. It was, she sobbed, the same bird which made its nest every year beneath the shelter of their outhouse﻿—she knew it quite well, and she showed him a red-coloured feather in its white breast. It had been struck dead by a single shot, and only a single red drop had come out of it; it had tried to reach its nest, but had died on its way on the strand. She wept as if her heart would break, and dried her face with her hair in impetuous Finnish fashion. Eilert laughed at her as boys will, but he overdid it, and was very pale the whole time. He dared not tell her that that very day he had taken a random shot with his father’s gun from behind the headland at a bird a long way off which was swimming ashore.

One autumn Eilert’s father was downright desperate. Day after day on the fishing grounds his lines caught next to nothing, while he was forced to look on and see the Finn pull up one rich catch after another. He was sure, too, that he had noticed malicious gestures over in the Finn’s boat. After that his whole house nourished a double bitterness against them; and when they talked it over in the evening, it was agreed, as a thing beyond all question, that Finnish sorcery had something to do with it. Against this there was only one remedy, and that was to rub corpse-mould on the lines; but one must beware of doing so, lest one should thereby offend the dead, and expose oneself to their vengeance, while the sea-folk would gain power over one at the same time.

===Chinese===
这一切都叫他冒火，因为他由不得自己喜欢山下那边的芬恩人家，尤其是小齐拉（Zilla）。他们俩总是厮守在一处：她知道那么多人鱼的事。从此，每当同她玩耍，良心便总要折磨他；每当她讲着故事、用那双黑色的大眼睛直直凝视他，他总会开始有点儿发怵，因为这种时候他便想起，她和她的族人属于受诅咒的沉沦之类，正因如此，他们才懂得那么多那样的事情。可反过来，一想到这个，他又恨得心头滴血，尤其是替她。她也常常被他这般古怪的做派弄得错愕，全然摸不着头脑；于是便照她的老脾气，笑着逗弄他，引他来追，自己却跑去藏起来。

有一天，他见她坐在海边一块大圆石上。她怀里抱着一只被打死的绒鸭，死了准没多久——身子还温热呢——她正为它哭得伤心。她抽抽噎噎地说，这就是年年在他们棚屋檐下做窝的那只鸭——她认得真真的——还指给他看那白色胸脯上的一根红羽毛。它被一枪毙命，身上只渗出孤零零一滴红血珠；它拼命想撑回窝里去，却半路死在了滩头上。她哭得肝肠寸断，拿芬兰人那种不管不顾的热烈劲儿，用自己的头发擦着脸。埃勒特像半大小子惯常的那样笑话她，只是笑得过了头，而且从头到尾脸色煞白。他不敢告诉她，就在那天，他曾躲在岬子后头，拿父亲的枪，朝远处一只正往岸边游的水鸟胡乱放过一枪。

有一年秋天，埃勒特的父亲简直绝望了。在渔场上，他的钓线日复一日几乎钓不着什么，却又不得不眼睁睁看着那个芬恩人一船接一船满载而起。他还一口咬定，自己瞅见了芬恩人船上那头有人比划恶毒的手势。打那以后，他全家对他们怀了双倍的怨毒；晚上一家人翻来覆去地合计，认定了一件无可置疑的事：这是芬兰巫术在作祟。对付它只有一样灵药，就是往钓线上抹尸土（corpse-mould）；只是干这个须得小心，免得因此冒犯了死者，招来他们的报复，同时又让海里的族类攥住辖制你的权柄。

===Original===
Eilert bothered his head a good deal over all this; it almost seemed to him as if he had had a share in the deed, because he was on such a good footing with the Finn folks.

On the following Sunday both he and the Finn folks were at Berg church, and he secretly abstracted a handful of mould from one of the Finn graves, and put it in his pocket. The same evening, when they came home, he strewed the mould over his father’s lines unobserved. And, oddly enough, the very next time his father cast his lines, as many fish were caught as in the good old times. But after this Eilert’s anxiety became indescribable. He was especially cautions while they were working of an evening round the fireside, and it was dark in the distant corners of the room. He sat there with a piece of steel in his pocket. To beg “forgiveness” of the dead is the only helpful means against the consequences of such deeds as his, otherwise one will be dragged off at night, by an invisible hand, to the churchyard, though one were lashed fast to the bed by a ship’s hawser.

When Eilert, on the following “Preaching Sunday,” went to church, he took very good care to go to the grave, and beg forgiveness of the dead.

As Eilert grew older, he got to understand that the Finn folks must, after all, be pretty much the same sort of people as his own folks at home; but, on the other hand, another thought was now uppermost in his mind, the thought, namely, that the Finns must be of an inferior stock, with a taint of disgrace about them. Nevertheless, he could not very well do without Zilla’s society, and they were very much together as before, especially at the time of their confirmation.

===Chinese===
这一切叫埃勒特费了好一番心思；他几乎觉得自己同那桩勾当也沾了份子，因为他同芬恩人交情这般好。

接下来的礼拜天，他和芬恩人家都在贝尔格教堂做礼拜，他偷偷从一座芬恩坟上抓了一把坟土，揣进兜里。当天傍晚回家后，他趁人不备，把那把土撒在了父亲的钓线上。说来也怪，父亲下一次下线，钓上来的鱼竟同从前的好年景一样多。可打这以后，埃勒特的心慌得没法形容。晚上一家人围着炉火做活、屋子远处的角落一片昏暗时，他尤其提心吊胆。他兜里揣着一小块钢。向死者乞求“饶恕”，是对他这等行径的后患唯一灵验的法子；不然，夜里就会有一只看不见的手把你拖到教堂墓地去，哪怕你用船上的粗缆把自己死死捆在床上，也逃不脱。

到了下一个“讲道主日（Preaching Sunday）”，埃勒特上教堂去的时候，特意寻到那座坟前，向死者乞求了饶恕。

埃勒特渐渐长大，才明白芬恩人到底同自己家里人差不多是一路人；可另一方面，另一个念头这时却占了上风，那就是：芬恩人终究是个低贱的族类，身上带着洗不净的污名。话虽如此，他又实在缺不得齐拉的相伴，两人照旧过从甚密，尤其是行坚信礼的那段日子。
===Original===
But when Eilert became a man, and mixed more with the people of the parish, he began to fancy that this old companionship lowered him somewhat in the eyes of his neighbours. There was nobody who did not believe as a matter of course that there was something shameful about Finn blood, and he, therefore, always tried to avoid her in company.

The girl understood it all well enough, for latterly she took care to keep out of his way. Nevertheless, one day she came, as had been her wont from childhood, down to their house, and begged for leave to go in their boat when they rowed to church next day. There were lots of strangers present from the village, and so Eilert, lest folks should think that he and she were engaged, answered mockingly, so that everyone could hear him, “that church-cleansing was perhaps a very good thing for Finnish sorcery,” but she must find someone else to ferry her across.

After that she never spoke to him at all, but Eilert was anything but happy in consequence.

===Chinese===
可等埃勒特长大成人，在教区的人群里走动得多了，他便觉得这段旧交情让自己在邻里眼里矮了一头。人人都理所当然地相信芬恩之血带着羞耻，因此一到人前，他总是设法躲她远远的。

姑娘心里全明白，因为近来她也留心躲着他了。尽管如此，有一天她还是照着从小以来的老习惯，下到他们家里来，求他们明天划船去教堂时捎她一个。屋里正有许多村里来的生人，埃勒特生怕人家以为他俩订了亲，便讥诮地作答，声音高得人人都听得见：“说教堂净化礼（church-cleansing）对付芬兰巫术，想必是桩大好事”——可她要渡海，总得另找旁人。

打那以后，她再没同他讲过一句话；可埃勒特心里也一点儿都不痛快。

===Original===
Now it happened one winter that Eilert was out all alone fishing for Greenland shark. A shark suddenly bit. The boat was small, and the fish was very big; but Eilert would not give in, and the end of the business was that his boat capsized.

All night long he lay on the top of it in the mist and a cruel sea. As now he sat there almost fainting for drowsiness, and dimly conscious that the end was not far off, and the sooner it came the better, he suddenly saw a man in seaman’s clothes sitting astride the other end of the boat’s bottom, and glaring savagely at him with a pair of dull reddish eyes. He was so heavy that the boat’s bottom began to slowly sink down at end where he sat. Then he suddenly vanished, but it seemed to Eilert as if the sea-fog lifted a bit; the sea had all at once grown quite calm (at least, there was now only a gentle swell); and right in front of him lay a little low grey island, towards which the boat was slowly drifting.

The skerry was wet, as if the sea had only recently been flowing over it, and on it he saw a pale girl with such lovely eyes. She wore a green kirtle, and round her body a broad silver girdle with figures upon it, such as the Finns use. Her bodice was of tar-brown skin, and beneath her stay-laces, which seemed to be of green sea-grass, was a foam-white chemise, like the feathery breast of a seabird.

===Chinese===
有一年冬天，埃勒特独自出海钓格陵兰鲨。一条鲨鱼猛地咬了钩。船小，鱼大；可埃勒特不肯撒手，到头来，船翻了。

整整一夜，他趴在翻扣的船底上，四周是海雾和狠毒的海。他坐在那儿，困得几乎昏死过去，隐隐觉得末日已经不远，而且来得越快越好；就在这时，他忽然看见一个水手装束的男人，跨坐在船底的另一头，用一双暗淡泛红的眼睛凶狠地瞪着他。那人沉重无比，船底让他坐的那一头渐渐往下沉去。随后他倏地不见了；埃勒特只觉海雾仿佛掀起了一角，海面陡然平静下来（至少，如今只剩一股缓缓的涌浪），正前方卧着一座低矮的灰色小礁岛，船正朝它慢慢漂去。

礁岛上湿漉漉的，仿佛海水方才还漫过它；岛子上立着一个面色苍白的姑娘，一双眼睛好看极了。她穿一条绿色长裙，腰间束一条錾着花纹的宽银腰带，正是芬恩人用的那种式样。她的紧身衣是焦油褐色的皮子，束胸的系带像是绿海草打的，带子底下露出一袭浪花般雪白的衬衣，白得像海鸟绒羽的胸脯。

===Original===
When the boat came drifting on to the island, she came down to him and said, as if she knew him quite well, “So you’re come at last, Eilert; I’ve been waiting for you so long!”

It seemed to Eilert as if an icy cold shudder ran through his body when he took the hand which helped him ashore; but it was only for the moment, and he forgot it instantly.

In the midst of the island there was an opening with a brazen flight of steps leading down to a splendid cabin. Whilst he stood there thinking things over a bit, he saw two heavy dogfish swimming close by﻿—they were, at least, twelve to fourteen ells long.

As they descended, the dogfish sank down too, each on one side of the brazen steps. Oddly enough, it looked as if the island was transparent. When the girl perceived that he was frightened, she told him that they were only two of her father’s bodyguard, and shortly afterwards they disappeared. She then said that she wanted to take him to her father, who was waiting for them. She added that, if he didn’t find the old gentleman precisely as handsome as he might expect, he had, nevertheless, no need to be frightened, nor was he to be astonished too much at what he saw.

===Chinese===
船漂到岛上时，她迎下来说道，俨然同他熟稔得很：“你可算来了，埃勒特；我等了你这么久！”

埃勒特握住那只扶他上岸的手，只觉一股冰冷的战栗贯穿全身；不过那只是一瞬，他转眼就忘了。

岛子中央有一个洞口，一道黄铜阶梯直通下去，通往一间华丽的舱房。他站在那儿略一思量的当口，看见两条沉甸甸的角鲨在近旁游弋——少说也有十二到十四厄尔（ell）长。

他们往下走时，那两条角鲨也跟着下沉，一边一条护在铜阶两侧。说来也怪，那岛子看上去竟是透明的。姑娘见他被吓着了，便告诉他，这两条不过是他父亲卫队里的两名护卫，话音落后不久，它们便不见了。她接着说，要带他去见父亲，父亲正等着他们。她又补了一句：要是他没觉得老先生全然如自己指望的那般体面，也大可不必害怕，对他看见的东西，也不必太过惊奇。
===Original===
He now perceived that he was under water, but, for all that, there was no sign of moisture. He was on a white sandy bottom, covered with chalk-white, red, blue, and silvery-bright shells. He saw meadows of sea-grass, mountains thick with woods of bushy seaweed and sea-wrack, and the fishes darted about on every side just as the birds swarm about the rocks that sea-fowl haunt.

As they two were thus walking along together she explained many things to him. High up he saw something which looked like a black cloud with a white lining, and beneath it moved backwards and forwards a shape resembling one of the dogfish.

“What you see there is a vessel,” said she; “there’s nasty weather up there now, and beneath the boat goes he who was sitting along with you on the bottom of the boat just now. If it is wrecked, it will belong to us, and then you will not be able to speak to father today.” As she said this there was a wild rapacious gleam in her eyes, but it was gone again immediately.

And, in point of fact, it was no easy matter to make out the meaning of her eyes. As a rule, they were unfathomably dark with the lustre of a night-billow through which the sea-fire sparkles; but, occasionally, when she laughed, they took a bright sea-green glitter, as when the sun shines deep down into the sea.

===Chinese===
他这才发觉自己已置身水底，然而浑身上下不见一丝水汽。他站在白色的沙底上，沙底铺满垩白、绯红、湛蓝和银亮的贝壳。他望见一片片海草的草甸，一座座大山，山上密密覆着蓬松海藻和漂积海草织成的丛林；鱼群四面八方地穿梭来往，恰似海鸟盘踞的礁岩四周翻飞的鸟阵。

两人就这样一路同行，她为他讲解种种事物。高处他看见一样东西，像一朵镶着白里的黑云，云底下有个影子来回游动，形状正像那两条角鲨中的一条。

“你在那儿看见的是一条船，”她说，“上头正闹着恶天气，船底下跟着走的，就是方才同你一起坐在船底上的那个。船要是沉了，就归我们了，那你今天也就见不成父亲了。”她说这话时，眼睛里掠过一丝狂野贪婪的光，但随即又敛去了。

说实在的，她那双眼睛的意思实在难猜。平日里，它们深黑得望不见底，泛着夜浪的幽光，浪中闪烁着海火；可偶尔她一笑，它们便亮出海水的碧莹莹的闪光，就像太阳直照进深海深处。

===Original===
Now and again they passed by a boat or a vessel half buried in the sand, out and in of the cabin doors and windows of which fishes swam to and fro. Close by the wrecks wandered human shapes which seemed to consist of nothing but blue smoke. His conductress explained to him that these were the spirits of drowned men who had not had Christian burial﻿—one must beware of them, for dead ones of this sort are malignant. They always know when one of their own race is about to be wrecked, and at such times they howl the death-warning of the Draug through the wintry nights.

Then they went further on their way right across a deep dark valley.
