Then he came to a hilly slope where the ferns stood high, and there were lots of birch bushes. It was so nice and shady there, he thought, and so he couldn’t for the life of him help taking a rest.

His drum he took off, his jacket he put beneath his head, and his cap over his face, and off he went to sleep.

But as he lay dozing there, he dreamt that someone was tickling him under the nose with a straw so that he could get no peace; and the instant he awoke, he fancied he heard laughing and giggling.

The sun had by this time begun to cast oblique shadows, and far down below, towards the valleys, lay the warm steaming vapours, creeping upwards in long drawn-out gossamer bands and ribbons of mist.

===Chinese===

随后他来到一道山坡，那里蕨草长得高高的，还有一丛丛的桦树。这儿又舒服又荫凉，他想，说什么也忍不住要歇上一歇。

他卸下鼓，外套垫在头底下，帽子盖在脸上，便自顾自睡去了。

可他正打着盹，梦见有人拿一根草茎在他鼻子底下挠痒痒，挠得他不得安生；他猛然醒来那一霎，恍惚听见一阵嬉笑和哧哧的笑声。

这时太阳已经开始斜照，投下歪歪的影子；远远的山下，朝着谷地的方向，卧着暖烘烘、蒸腾腾的雾气，化作长长的游丝带子和雾霭的缎带，慢慢往上爬。

===Original===

As he reached behind him for his jacket, he saw a snake, which lay and looked at him with such shrewd quick eyes. But when he threw a stone at it, it caught its tail in its mouth, and trundled away like a wheel.

Again there was a giggling and a sniggering among the bushes.

And now he heard it among some birch trees which stood in such a wonderful sunlight, for they were filled with the rain and fine drizzle of a waterfall. The water-drops glistened and sparkled so that he really couldn’t see the trees properly.

But it was as though something were moving about in them, and he could have sworn that he had caught a glimpse of a fine bright slim damsel, who was laughing and making fun of him. She peeped at him from beneath her hand, because of the sun, and her sleeves were tucked up.

===Chinese===

他伸手到身后去够外套，忽见一条蛇卧在那里，正用那般机敏迅捷的眼睛望着他。可等他扔过一块石头，那蛇便一口衔住自己的尾巴，像轮子似的滚走了。

树丛里又是一阵哧哧的窃笑。

这回他听见笑声在几棵桦树之间，那些树立在一片奇异的阳光里，因为满树都是瀑布溅来的水珠和蒙蒙细雨般的水雾。水珠晶莹闪烁，晃得他简直看不清那些树。

可树丛里仿佛有什么东西在动来动去，他敢起誓，他瞥见了一个明媚苗条的少女，正笑着拿他取乐。她因为阳光晃眼，把手遮在眉上偷觑他，袖子挽得高高的。

===Original===

A little while afterwards a dark-blue blouse appeared above the brushwood.

He was after it in an instant.

He ran and ran till he had half a mind to give up, but then a frock and a bare shoulder gleamed betwixt an opening in the leaves.

And off again he pelted as hard as he could, till he began to think that it must have all been imagination.

Then he saw her right in a corner of the green bushes. Her hair had been torn out of its plaits from the speed with which she had flown through the bushes. She stood still, and looked back as if she were terribly frightened.

===Chinese===

过了一小会儿，灌木丛上方露出了一件深蓝色的罩衫。

他转眼就追了上去。

他跑啊跑啊，跑到几乎想撒手不追了，可正在这时，树叶的一个缝隙间又闪出一袭长裙和一只裸露的肩膀。

于是他又没命地猛追起来，追到后来，他开始觉得这八成全是他自己想出来的。

忽然，他果真在绿灌木的一个角落里看见了她。她在树丛间飞窜得那样快，头发都从发辫里扯散了。她站住脚，回过头来望着，仿佛吓得不轻。

===Original===

But the lad thought to himself that if she had run away with his drumsticks, she should pay for it.

And off they ran again, she in front, and he behind.

Now and again she turned round and laughed and gibed, and gave a toss and a twist, so that it looked as if her long wavy hair were writhing and wriggling and twisting like a serpent’s tail.

At last she turned round on the top of the hill, laughed, and held out his drumsticks towards him.

===Chinese===

可小伙子心里想，她若是拿了他的鼓槌跑掉，就得为此付出代价。

于是他们又跑了起来，她在前，他在后。

她时不时回过头来笑着挖苦他，腰身一拧一摆，那头长长的波浪似的长发便随之翻卷、扭动、缠绕，活像一条蛇的尾巴。

最后她在山顶上转过身来，笑着把他的鼓槌朝他递了过来。

===Original===

But now he was determined to catch her. He was so near that he made grab after grab at her; but just as he was about to lay hold of her hard by a fence, she was over it, while he tumbled after her into the enclosure of a homestead.

Then she cried and shouted up to the house, “Randi, and Brandi, and Gyri, and Gunna!”

And four girls came rushing down over the sward.

But the last of them, who had a fine ruddy complexion and heavy golden-red hair, stood and greeted him so graciously with her downcast eyes, as if she was quite distressed that they should play such wanton pranks with a strange young man.

===Chinese===

可这回他打定了主意要抓住她。他离得那么近，一把又一把地朝她抓去；可就在栅栏边上他快要揪住她的时候，她已翻了过去，他却跟着她一个趔趄，跌进了一处农庄的园子里。

于是她朝着上面的房子喊叫起来：“兰迪（Randi），布兰迪（Brandi），居丽（Gyri），还有贡娜（Gunna）！”

四个姑娘飞奔着跑下草地来。

只是最后一个姑娘，她脸色红润秀美，一头厚厚的金红头发，垂着眼睛文文静静地向他问好，仿佛她们对一个外乡小伙子这般轻狂撒野，叫她心里十分过意不去。

===Original===

She stood there abashed and uncertain, poor thing! just like a child, who knows not whether it should say something or not; but all the while she sidled up nearer and nearer to him. Then, when she was so close to him that her hair almost touched him, she opened her blue eyes wide, and looked straight at him.

But she had a frightfully sharp look in those eyes of hers.

“Rather come with me, and thou shalt have dancing﻿—or art thou tired, my lad?” cried a girl with blue-black hair, and a wild dark fire in her eyes. She tripped up and down, and clapped her hands. She had white teeth and hot breath, and would have dragged him off with her.

===Chinese===

她站在那里，又羞怯又局促，怪可怜的！活像个孩子，不知道该不该开口说话；可她一边却挨挨蹭蹭，离他越来越近。等到她凑得那样近，头发几乎碰着他了，她忽然睁开那双蓝眼睛，直勾勾地望进他眼里。

可她那双眼睛里的目光，尖利得吓人。

“还不如跟我来，让你跳个痛快——还是说累了，我的小子？”一个黑蓝色头发、眼里燃着狂野暗火的姑娘喊道。她轻盈地走来走去，拍着巴掌。她牙齿雪白，气息滚烫，简直要连拖带拽把他弄走。

===Original===

“Tie thyself up behind first, black Gyri!” giggled the others.

And immediately she let the lad go, and wobbled and twisted, and went backwards so oddly.

He couldn’t help staring after the black lassie, who stood and writhed and twisted so uncomfortably, as if she were concealing something behind her, and had, all at once, become so meek.

But the fine bright girl with the slim slender waist, who had rushed on before him, and who seemed to him the loveliest of them all, began to laugh at him again and tease him.

===Chinese===

“先把你身后捆扎好了再说吧，黑居丽！”其余的姑娘哧哧笑道。

她立刻放开了小伙子，扭扭摆摆、歪歪斜斜，倒退着走开，那模样好不古怪。

他不由得盯着那黑发姑娘直瞧，只见她站在那里局促不安地扭来扭去，仿佛身后藏着什么物件，又忽然一下子变得那么温驯。

可那位明媚俏丽、腰肢纤细的姑娘——就是先前在他前头飞跑、在他眼里也是她们中间最可爱的那一个——又冲着他笑闹起来，逗弄起他来。

===Original===

Run as he might, he shouldn’t catch her, she jibed and jeered; never should he find his drumsticks again, she said.

But then her mood shifted right round, and she flung herself down headlong, and began to cry. She had followed his drumming the whole day, she said, and never had she heard any fellow who could beat *rat-tat-tat* so well; nor had she ever seen a lad who was so handsome while he was asleep. “I kissed thee then,” said she, and smiled up at him so sorrowfully.

“Beware of the serpent’s tongue, lest it bite thee, swain! ’Tis worst of all when it licks thee first,” whispered the bashful one with the golden-red hair. She would fain have stolen between them so softly.

And all at once the swain recollected the snake, which was as slender, and supple, and quick, and sparkling as the girl who lay there on the hillside, and wept and made fun at the same time and looked oddly alert and wary.

===Chinese===

他再怎么跑也休想追上她，她这样讥笑嘲弄；他的鼓槌么，永远也别想再找回来了，她说。

可忽然间她的心思整个翻转过来，一头扑倒在地上，哭了起来。她听他的鼓声听了一整天，她说，从没听见哪个小子能把“咚哒哒”敲得这么好；她也从没见过哪个小伙子睡着的时候有这般俊。“那时候我还亲了你一口呢，”她说着，抬起眼朝他凄然一笑。

“当心那蛇的信子，免得它咬着你，情郎！它先舔你一口，那才最毒呢。”那位金红头发、脸皮薄的姑娘低声说。她多想轻轻地、悄悄地挤到他们两人中间去。

小伙子猛然想起了那条蛇——它也这般细长、柔韧、迅捷、闪光，正像这个躺在山坡上的姑娘，一边哭一边闹，一双眼却警觉得蹊跷。

===Original===

But a stooping, somewhat clumsy little thing now stuck her head quickly in between, and smiled shamefacedly at him, as if she knew and could tell him so much. Her eyes sparkled a long way inwards, and across her face there passed a sort of pale golden gleam, as when the last sunbeam slowly draws away from the grassy mountain slope.

“At my place,” said she, “thou shalt hear such *Langelejk*39 as none else has ever heard. I will play for thee, and thou shalt listen to things unknown to others. Thou shalt hear all that sings, and laughs, and cries in the roots of trees, and in the mountains, and in all things that grow, so that thou wilt never trouble thy head about anything else in the world.”

Then there was a scornful laugh; and up on a rock he saw a tall strongly built girl, with a gold band in her hair and a huge wand in her hand.

She lifted a long wooden trumpet with such splendid powerful arms, threw back her neck with such a proud and resolute air, and stood firm and fast as a rock while she blew.

===Chinese===

可这会儿一个佝偻着背、有点笨拙的小个子姑娘，忽然把脑袋从中间探了进来，红着脸朝他微笑，仿佛她什么都知道，有一肚子话能讲给他听。她的眼睛在幽深处闪着光，脸上掠过一抹淡淡的金黄，就像最后一缕夕阳从青草山坡上缓缓收去时的光景。

“到了我家，”她说，“你会听到谁也没听过的朗格莱克琴（Langelejk）39。我要弹给你听，你会听到旁人无从知晓的事情。你会听见树根里、群山里、一切生长的万物里那些歌唱、欢笑和哭泣的声音，从此这世上别的事情，你再也不会放在心上了。”

接着响起一声轻蔑的笑；只见一块岩石上立着一个高挑健壮的姑娘，发间束着金带，手里拄着一根大手杖。

她用那双健美有力的臂膀举起一支长长的木号，昂起脖颈，神情那般骄傲果决，吹奏的时候站得稳稳当当，纹丝不动，就像岩石一样。

===Original===

And it sounded far and wide through the summer evening, and rang back again across the hills.

But she, the prettiest and daintiest of them all, who had cast herself on the ground, stuck her fingers in her ears, and mimicked her and laughed and jeered.

Then she glanced up at him with her blue eyes peeping through her ashen-yellow hair, and whispered﻿—

“If thou dost want me, swain, thou must pick me up.”

===Chinese===

号声在夏天的黄昏里传得很远很远，又一路回响着荡过群山。

可她——她们中间最娇美最秀气的那一个，正扑在地上——把手指塞进耳朵里，学她的样儿，又是笑又是嘲。

随后她抬眼望他，蓝眼睛从灰黄的头发缝里觑出来，低声说——

“你要是想要我，情郎，就得把我从地上抱起来。”

===Original===

“She has a strong firm grip for a gentle maiden,” thought he to himself, as he raised her from the ground.

“But thou must catch me first,” cried she.

And right towards the house they ran﻿—she first, and he after her.

Suddenly she stopped short, and putting both arms akimbo, looked straight into his eyes: “Dost like me?” she asked.

===Chinese===

“一位娇滴滴的姑娘，手劲儿倒真不小，”他把她从地上抱起来的时候，心里这样想。

“可你得先抓住我才行，”她喊道。

两人直朝那座房子奔去——她在前头，他在后面追。

冷不防她收住脚，双手叉腰，直直望进他的眼睛：“你喜欢我么？”她问。

===Original===

The swain couldn’t say no to that. He had now got hold of her, and would have put his arm round her.

“ ’Tis for thee to have a word in the matter, father,” she shouted all at once in the direction of the house; “this swain here would fain wed me.”

And she drew him hastily towards the hut door.

There sat a little grey-clad old fellow, with a cap like a milk-can on his head, staring at the livestock on the mountainside. He had a large silver jug in front of him.

===Chinese===

这个问题，小伙子怎么也说不出个“不”字。这会儿他已经抓住了她，正想伸胳膊搂住她。

“这件事该你老人家说句话了，爹，”她忽然朝着房子的方向喊道；“这位情郎一心想娶我呢。”

于是她急急忙忙地把他往小屋门口拽去。

那边坐着一个穿灰衣裳的小老头，头上扣着一顶牛奶罐似的帽子，正望着山坡上的牲口出神。他面前摆着一只大银壶。

===Original===

“ ’Tis the homestead westward in the Blue Mountains that he’s after, I know,” said the old man, nodding his head, with a sly look in his eyes.

“Haw, haw! That’s what they’re after, is it?” thought the swain. But aloud he said, “ ’Tis a great offer, I know; but methinks ’tis a little hasty too. Down our way ’tis the custom to send two go-betweens first of all to arrange matters properly.”

“Thou *didst* send two before thee, and here they be,” quoth she smartly, and produced his drumsticks.

“And ’tis usual with us, moreover, to have a look over the property first; though the lass herself have wit enough and to spare,” added he.

===Chinese===

“他打的是蓝山（Blue Mountains）以西这座农庄的主意，我心里明白，”老头说着点点头，眼睛里透出一丝狡黠。

“嚯，嚯！原来他们打的是这个主意，是么？”小伙子心想。可嘴上他说：“这是份厚礼，我知道；不过我看也着实仓促了些。我们那边讲究先请两位媒人上门，把事情体体面面地议妥。”

“你先前不是先打发两个来了么，喏，就在这儿呢，”她伶牙俐齿地答道，一面把他的鼓槌亮了出来。

“再说按我们的规矩，总还得先相看相看家产；虽说姑娘本人就伶俐得够有余了，”他又添了一句。

===Original===

Then she all at once grew so small, and there was a nasty green glitter in her eyes﻿—

“Hast thou not run after me the livelong day, and wooed me right down in the enclosure there, so that my father both heard and saw it all?” cried she.

“Pretty lasses are wont to hold back a bit,” said the swain, in a wheedling sort of way. He perceived that he must be a little subtle here; it was not all love in this wooing.

Then she seemed to bend her body backwards into a complete curve, and shot forward her head and neck, and her eyes sparkled.

===Chinese===

这时她忽然一下子缩得那么小，眼睛里闪出一股恶狠狠的绿光——

“你不是追了我一整天，就在下面园子里当众求的亲，我爹听得清清楚楚、看得明明白白么？”她嚷道。

“俊姑娘家总要先拿捏一番嘛，”小伙子哄劝似的说。他觉察到这里头得耍点心眼；这场求亲里搀的可不全是情意。

她仿佛把身子向后弯成一张满弓，脑袋和脖颈倏地朝前一探，双眼灼灼放光。

===Original===

But the old fellow lifted his stick from his knee, and she stood there again as blithe and sportive as ever.

She stretched herself out tall and stiff, with her hands in her silver girdle; and she looked right into his eyes and laughed, and asked him if he was one of those fellows who were afraid of the girls. If he wanted her he might perchance be run off his legs again, said she.

Then she began tripping up and down, and curtseying and making fun of him again.

But all at once he saw on the sward behind her what looked like the shadow of something that whisked and frisked and writhed round and round, and twisted in and out according as she practised her wheedling ways upon him.

===Chinese===

可老头把搁在膝上的手杖一抬，她便又站在那里，欢蹦乱跳，一如方才。

她把自己抻得又高又僵直，双手插在银腰带上；她直望着他的眼睛笑，问他是不是那种怕姑娘的小子。她还说，他要是想要她，没准儿还得叫她再跑断他的腿。

接着她又踏着轻步走过来走过去，一边行屈膝礼，一边重新拿他打趣。

可忽然间，他看见她身后的草地上，有个什么东西的影子，甩来甩去、翻卷扭动，随着她对他使出千娇百媚的手段，那影子也穿进穿出、盘绕不定。

===Original===

“That is a very curious long sort of ribbon,” thought the drummer to himself in his amazement. They were in a great hurry, too, to get him under the yoke, he thought; but they should find that a soldier on his way to the manoeuvres is not to be betrothed and married offhand.

So he told them bluntly that he had come hither for his drumsticks, and not to woo maidens, and he would thank them to let him have his property.

“But have a look about you a bit first, young man,” said the old fellow, and he pointed with his stick.

And all at once the drummer saw large dun cows grazing all along the mountain pastures, and the cowbells rang out their merry peals. Buckets and vats of the brightest copper shone all about, and never had he seen such shapely and nicely dressed milkmaids. There must needs be great wealth here.

===Chinese===

“好一条古里古怪的长缎带，”鼓手暗自纳罕。他们急着把他套上轭，这也太心切了，他想；可他们也该明白，一个赶去操练的士兵，是不能随随便便说订亲就订亲、说成亲就成亲的。

于是他直截了当地告诉他们，他是来取鼓槌的，不是来求姑娘的，还请他们把他的东西还给他。

“可你先四下里瞧一眼再说，年轻人，”老头说着，用手杖指了指。

鼓手忽然看见满山的牧场上到处放牧着灰褐色的大奶牛，牛铃铛叮叮当当，敲出欢畅的调子。四下里到处亮闪闪的，全是光可鉴人的铜桶铜盆；还有那些挤奶女，一个个身段窈窕、装束齐整，他生平从没见过这样的。这里头必定藏着偌大的家业。

===Original===

“Perchance thou dost think ’tis but a beggarly inheritance I have here in the Blue Mountains,” said she, and sitting down on a haycock, she began chatting with him. “But we’ve four such *sætar*40 as this, and what I inherit from my mother is twelve times as large.”

But the drummer had seen what he had seen. They were rather too anxious to settle the property upon him, thought he. So he declared that in so serious a matter he must crave a little time for consideration.

Then the lass began to cry and take on, and asked him if he meant to befool a poor innocent, ignorant, young thing, and pursue her and drive her out of her very wits. She had put all her hope and trust in him, she said, and with that she fell a-howling.

She sat there quite inconsolable, and rocked herself to and fro with all her hair over her eyes, till at last the drummer began to feel quite sorry for her and almost angry with himself. She was certainly most simple-minded and confiding.

===Chinese===

“你许是觉得，我在蓝山里的这份家当寒碜得很吧，”她说着，在一个干草垛上坐下来，同他攀谈起来。“可这样的夏季牧场（sætar）40，我们有四处，我从我娘那儿继承的，比这还要大上十二倍呢。”

可鼓手该看见的都看见了。他们急着把这份家产往他头上安，未免太心切了些，他想。于是他声言，这般终身大事，他总得讨些时间考虑考虑。

姑娘顿时哭闹起来，问他是不是存心作弄她这样一个可怜无辜、少不更事的嫩丫头，追着她，把她逼得几乎发疯。她把全部的希望和信托都押在他身上了，她说，说到这儿便嚎啕大哭起来。

她坐在那里哭得无法安慰，头发披散了一脸，身子前仰后合地摇晃，到后来鼓手竟觉得怪可怜她，倒几乎要生自己的气了。她实在是天真烂漫，一副全然信人的样子。

===Original===

All at once she twisted round and threw herself petulantly down from the haycock. Her eyes spied all about, and seemed quite tiny and piercing as she looked up at him, and laughed and jested.

He started back. It was exactly as if he again saw the snake beneath the birch tree down there when it trundled away.

And now he wanted to be off as quickly as possible; he cared no longer about being civil.

Then she reared up with a hissing sound. She quite forgot herself, and a long tail hung down and whisked about from behind her kirtle.

===Chinese===

冷不防她一扭身，赌着气从草垛上扑了下来。她的眼睛四下里窥探，抬眼望他时显得那么细小、那么尖利，一面笑，一面打趣。

他吓得倒退了一步。这光景，恰像他又看见了下面那棵桦树底下衔尾滚走的蛇。

这下他只想尽快脱身，客套不客套也顾不得了。

她嘶的一声直起了身子。她全然忘形了，一条长尾巴从裙子后头耷拉下来，甩来甩去。

===Original===

He shouldn’t escape her in that way, she shrieked. He should first of all have a taste of public penance and public opinion from parish to parish. And then she called her father.

Then the drummer felt a grip on his jacket, and he was lifted right off his legs.

He was chucked into an empty cow-house, and the door was shut behind him.

There he stood and had nothing to look at but an old billy-goat through a crack in the door, who had odd, yellow eyes, and was very much like the old fellow, and a sunbeam through a little hole, which sunbeam crept higher and higher up the blank stable wall till late in the evening, when it went out altogether.

===Chinese===

想这样从她手心里逃掉，休想，她尖声喊道。他得先尝尝当众悔罪的滋味，领教一个教区接着一个教区的悠悠众口。说着她把她的爹喊了来。

鼓手只觉得衣襟被一把攥住，双脚腾空，整个人被提了起来。

他被扔进一间空牛棚，门在身后砰地关上了。

他站在那里，除了从门缝里望见一头老公羊——它长着一双古怪的黄眼睛，跟那老头像极了——再没有别的可看；还有透过一个小孔射进来的一线日光，顺着光秃秃的棚壁越爬越高，直到夜深才完全熄灭。

===Original===

But towards night a voice outside said softly, “Swain! swain!” and in the moonlight he saw a shadow cross the little hole.

“Hist! hist! the old man is sleeping at the other side of the wall,” it sounded.

He knew by the voice that it was she, the golden-red one, who had behaved so prettily and been so bashful the moment he had come upon the scene.

“Thou need’st but say that thou dost know that serpent-eye has had a lover before, or they wouldn’t be in such a hurry to get her off their hands with a dowry. Thou must know that the homestead westwards in the Blue Mountains is mine. And answer the old man that it was me, Brandi, that thou didst run after all the time. Hist! hist! here comes the old man,” she whispered, and whisked away.

===Chinese===

可是入夜时分，外头有个声音轻轻喊道：“情郎！情郎！”月光里，他看见一道影子掠过那个小孔。

“嘘！嘘！老头在墙那一边睡着呢，”那声音说。

听嗓音他就知道是她，那个金红头发的，他刚一露面时她举止那么端好，脸皮那么嫩。

“你只消说，你知道那蛇眼姑娘早有过一个情人了，不然他们哪会这么急，急着赔上嫁妆把她打发出门。你要知道，蓝山以西这座农庄是我的。你就回老头说，你一直追的是我布兰迪。嘘！嘘！老头来了，”她低声说完，一闪身去了。

===Original===

But a shadow again fell across the little hole in the moonlight, and the duck-necked one stuck her head in and peeped at him.

“Swain, swain, art thou awake?”

“That serpent-eye will make thee the laughingstock of the neighbourhood. She’s spiteful, and she stings. But the homestead westward in the Blue Mountains is mine, and when I play there the gates beneath the high mountains fly open, and through them lies the road to the nameless powers of nature. Do but say that ’twas me, Randi, thou wert running after, because she plays so prettily on the *Langelijk*.﻿—Hist, hist! the old man is stirring about by the wall!”﻿—she beckoned to him and was gone.

A little afterwards nearly every bit of the hole was darkened, and he recognised the Black one by her voice.

===Chinese===

可月光里又有一道影子落在小孔上，那个鸭脖子姑娘把头探进来，朝他窥望。

“情郎，情郎，你醒着么？”

“那个蛇眼姑娘会叫你成为左近一带的笑柄。她心肠歹毒，还会蜇人。可蓝山以西的农庄是我的，我一在那儿弹琴，高山底下的门户便豁然洞开，门里便是通往自然界无名伟力的路。你只消说，你追的是我兰迪，因为我兰迪的朗格莱克琴弹得那么动听。——嘘，嘘！老头在墙根边走动了！”——她朝他招了招手，去了。

过了不大一会儿，小孔差不多整个儿黑了，他听嗓音认出是那位黑姑娘。

===Original===

“Swain, swain!” she hissed.

“I had to bind up my kirtle today behind,” said she, “so we couldn’t go dancing the *Halling-fling*41 together on the green sward. But the homestead in the Blue Mountains is my lawful property, and tell the old man that it was madcap Gyri thou wast running after today, because thou art so madly fond of dancing jigs and *hallings*.”

Then she clapped her hands aloud, and straightway was full of fear lest she should have awakened the old man.

And she was gone.

===Chinese===

“情郎，情郎！”她嘶嘶地说。

“今天我只好把裙子在后头捆起来，”她说，“害得咱们不能一起在绿草地上跳哈灵飞旋舞（Halling-fling）41。可蓝山里的农庄是我名正言顺的产业。你告诉老头，你今天追的是疯丫头居丽，因为你最爱跳吉格舞和哈灵舞，跳得都发狂了。”

说着她响亮地拍起手来，随即又满心害怕，生怕吵醒了老头。

于是她也走了。

===Original===

But the lad sat inside there, and thought it all over, and looked up at the thin pale summer moon, and he thought that never in his whole life had he been in such evil case.

From time to time he heard something moving, scraping, and snorting against the wall outside. It was the old fellow who lay there and kept watch over him.

“Thou, swain, thou,” said another voice at the peephole.

It was she who had planted herself so firmly on the rock with such sturdy hips and such a masterful voice.

===Chinese===

小伙子坐在里面，把这一切前前后后想了一遍，抬头望着那轮薄薄的、颜色苍白的夏月，心想自己这辈子从来没落到过这般凶险的境地。

他不时听见有什么东西在墙外头动来动去，蹭啊，挠啊，还喷着鼻息。是那老头卧在外头守着他。

“你，情郎，你啊，”窥孔边又响起另一个声音。

是她，那个在岩石上站得那样稳、腰胯那样结实、嗓音那样霸道的姑娘。

===Original===

“For these three hundred years have I been blowing the *langelur42* here in the summer evenings far and wide, but never has it drawn anyone westward hither into the Blue Mountains. And let me tell thee that we are all homeless and houseless, and all thou seest here is but glitter and glamour. Many a man has been befooled hither time out of mind. But I won’t have the other lasses married before me. And rather than that any one of them should get thee, I’ll free thee from the mountains. Mark me, now! When the sun is hot and high the old man will get frightened and crawl into his corner. Then look to thyself. Shove hard against the door of the hayloft, and hasten to get thee over the fence, and thou wilt be rid of us.”

The drummer was not slow to follow this counsel. He crept out the moment the sun began to burn, and cleared the fence with one good bound.

In less than no time he was down in the valley again.

And far, far away towards sunrise in the mountains, he heard the sound of her *langelur*.

He threw his drum across his shoulder, and hied him off to the manoeuvres at Moen.

But never would he play *rat-tat-tat* and beat the tattoo before the lasses again, lest he should find himself westwards in the Blue Mountains before he was well aware of it.

===Chinese===

“三百年来，我一直在夏天的黄昏里朝四面八方吹这长卢尔号（langelur）42，却从来没有把一个人引到西边蓝山里来。实话告诉你吧，我们全都是无家无舍的人，你在这里看见的一切，不过是虚光幻彩。打不知多少年起，就总有人被骗到这儿来。可我不让别的姑娘抢在我前头出嫁。与其让她们哪一个得了你，我宁愿放你出山。你给我记好了！等日头毒、日头高的时候，老头就会害怕，缩进他的角落里去。那时你就见机行事。使劲推开草料棚的门，赶紧翻过栅栏，你就能摆脱我们了。”

鼓手毫不迟疑，照这主意办了。日头一开始发烫，他就爬了出来，一个漂亮的纵跃翻过了栅栏。

一转眼他又下到了谷地里。

在群山里远远的、远远的朝着日出的方向，他听见了她长卢尔号的声音。

他把鼓往肩上一挎，赶往默恩操练去了。

只是从此他再也不肯在姑娘们面前敲“咚哒哒”、打归营鼓了，生怕一个不留意，自己又到了蓝山以西的地方。

## The Wind-Gnome / 风精

===Original===

There was once a skipper of Dyrevig called Bardun. He was so headstrong that there was no doing anything with him. Whatever he set his mind upon, that should be done, he said, and done it always was.

If he promised to be at a dance, the girls could safely rely upon his being there, though it blew a tempest and rained cats and dogs.

He would come scudding along on a *Færing*36 to his father’s house through storm and stress. Row upon row of girls would be waiting for him there, and he spanked the floor with every one of them in turn, and left their gallants to cool their heels as best they might.

Cock-of-the-walk he always must be.

He would go shark-fishing too, and would venture with his fishing gaff into seas where only large vessels were wont to go.

If there was anything nobody else dared do, Bardun was the man to do it. And, absurd and desperate as the venture might be, he always succeeded, so that folks were always talking about him.

===Chinese===

从前，迪勒维克（Dyrevig）有个船长，名叫巴尔杜恩（Bardun）。他固执得厉害，谁也拿他没法子。他说，凡是他打定主意要做的事，就非做成不可——而那些事也从来件件都做成了。

他若答应去舞会，姑娘们尽可放心等他到场，哪怕外面刮着狂风、下着倾盆大雨。

任凭风狂雨骤，他总会驾着一艘法灵艇（Færing）36，一路飞驶赶回父亲家中。那里总有一排排姑娘等着他，他便挨个儿同她们跳，跳得地板咚咚作响，把姑娘们的情郎撇在一旁，让他们爱怎么干等就怎么干等。

他天生就非要处处称雄不可。

他还去捕鲨，敢带着搭钩闯进素来只有大船才去的海域。

只要有谁也不敢做的事，做这事的必定是巴尔杜恩。而且那营生再荒唐、再玩命，他总能得手，于是人们嘴里总也离不开他。

===Original===

Now, right out at sea, beyond the skerries, lay a large rock, the lair of wildfowl, whither the merchant who owned it came every year to bring away rich loads of eiderdown. A long way down the side of this lofty rock was a cleft. Nobody could tell how far *into* the rock it went, and so inaccessible was it there that its owner had said that whoever liked might come and take eiderdown from thence. It became quite a proverb to say, when anything couldn’t be done, that it was just as impossible as taking eiderdown from Dyrevig rock.

But Bardun passed by the rock, and peeped up at the cleft, and saw all the hosts of the fowls of the air lighting upon it so many times that he felt he needs must try his hand at it.

He lost no time about it, and the sun was shining brightly as he set out.

He took with him a long piece of rope, which he cast two or three times round a rocky crag, and lowered himself down till he was right opposite the cleft. There he hung and swung over it backwards and forwards till he had got a firm footing, and then he set about collecting eiderdown and stuffing his sacks with it.

===Chinese===

且说在外海上，礁岛群以外，卧着一座大岩，是野禽栖聚的巢窟，岩主商人年年前来，运走一船船丰厚的绒鸭绒。这高高的岩壁下方很低的地方，有一道裂罅。没人说得清它究竟往岩体深处延伸多远；那地方又偏偏无法企及，岩主便放话说，谁若有本事，尽管来取那儿的绒鸭绒。后来每逢有办不成的事，人们便有一句现成的俗话——那事就跟上迪勒维克岩采绒鸭绒一样，办不到。

可巴尔杜恩一回回打岩旁经过，仰头窥看那道裂罅，眼见空中的禽鸟成群成群一次次栖落其上，终于心头发痒，觉得非去试一手不可。

他毫不耽搁；动身那天，日头正照得明亮晃眼。

他带上一条长绳，在岩尖上绕了两三匝，攀绳而下，一直落到正对裂罅的地方。他就悬在那里，在罅前荡过来荡过去，直到立稳了脚跟，这才动手采集绒鸭绒，往一只只袋子里塞。

===Original===

He went searching about for it so far into the rocky chasm that he saw no more than a gleam of sunlight outside the opening, and he couldn’t take a hundreth part of the eiderdown that was there.

It was quite late in the evening before he gave up trying to gather it all. But when he came out again, the stone which he had placed on the top of the rope and tied it to was gone. And now the rope hung loosely there, and dangled over the side of the rock. The wind blew it in and out and hither and thither. The currents of air sported madly with it, so that it always kept sheer away from the rock and far out over the abyss.

There he stood then, and tried again and again to clutch hold of it till the sun lay right down in the sea.

When it began to dawn again, and the morning breeze rose up from the sea, he all at once heard something right over his head say﻿—

===Chinese===

他循着绒毛一路寻进岩隙，深到回望洞口，外面只剩一缕日光；那里面藏着的绒鸭绒，他连百分之一也拿不完。

直到夜深，他才死了心，不再想把绒毛收尽。可等他再钻出洞来，原先压在绳顶上系绳的那块石头竟不见了。绳子松松地悬在那儿，从岩边荡下去。风把它吹得忽进忽出、忽东忽西。股股气流拿它疯也似的耍弄，它总是笔直地荡离岩壁，远远悬在深渊上空。

他便立在那里，一遍又一遍地伸手去抓，直到太阳直落进海里。

待到天色又破晓，晨风从海上刮起，他蓦地听见头顶正上方有什么东西说道——

===Original===

“It blows away, it blows away!”

He looked up, and there he saw a big woman holding the rope away from the cliff side.

Every time he made a grip at it she wrenched and twisted it right away over the rocky wall, and there was a laughing and a grinning all down the mountain side﻿—

“It blows away, it blows away!”

And, again and again, the rope drove in and out and hither and thither.

===Chinese===

“要吹走啦，要吹走啦！”

他抬头一望，只见一个大个子女人，正把绳子从崖边拽开。

他每回伸手去抓，她便把绳子一拧一扯，整个拽过石壁那头去；满山坡顿时一片嬉笑、一片龇牙咧嘴——

“要吹走啦，要吹走啦！”

而那绳子便一次又一次，忽进忽出、忽东忽西。

===Original===

“You had better take a spring at once, and not wait till you’re tired,” thought he.

It was a pretty long leap to take, but he went back a sufficient distance, and then out he sprang.

Bardun was not the man to fall short of anything. He caught the rope and held it tight.

And, oddly enough, it seemed now to run up the cliff-side of its own accord, just as if someone were hoisting it.

===Chinese===

“索性趁早纵身一跳，别等累得没了力气。”他心想。

这一跳委实不近，可他往后退足了距离，随即纵身跃出。

巴尔杜恩岂是功亏一篑的人。他一把抓住绳子，攥得死紧。

说来也怪，这会儿绳子竟自个儿顺着崖壁往上跑，仿佛有什么人在上头往上拽似的。

===Original===

But in front of the rocky crag to which he had fastened the rope, he heard a soughing and a sighing, and something said, “I am the daughter of the Wind-Gnome, and now thou hast dominion over me! When the blast blows and whines about thee ’tis I who long for thee. And here thou hast a rudder which will give thee luck and a fair wind whithersoever thou farest. He who is with thee shall thrive, and he who is against thee shall suffer shipwreck and be lost. For ’tis I who am in the windy gusts.”

Then all at once everything was quite still; but down on the sea below there swept a heavy squall.

There stood Bardun with the rudder in his hand, and he understood that it was not a thing to be lightly cast away.

Homeward he steered with a racing breeze behind him, and he had not sailed far before he met a galeas which gave him the Bergen price for his eiderdown.

===Chinese===

可就在他系绳的那座岩尖前，他听见一阵呜咽、一声长叹，有什么东西说道：“我乃风精（Wind-Gnome）之女，如今你已主宰了我！每当狂风在你周身呼啸悲鸣，那是我思念你。这里有一柄舵赠你：无论你驶向何方，它都保你走运、一路顺风。助你的人必得兴旺，与你作对的人必遭船难、沦亡海上。因为我就在那阵阵疾风里。”

随即四下里骤然一片死寂；唯有下方的海面上，扫过一阵沉重的狂风。

巴尔杜恩手握那柄舵立在那里，心里明白，这可不是能随手丢弃的东西。

他掌舵归家，疾风在后；没驶出多远，便遇上一艘加利亚斯帆船（galeas），照卑尔根（Bergen）的价钱买下了他的绒鸭绒。

===Original===

But Bardun was not content with only going thither once. He went just the same as before, and he returned from the Dyrevig rock with a pile of sacks of eiderdown on his boat right up to the mast.

He bought houses and ships; mightier and mightier he grew.

And it was not long before he owned whole fishing-grounds, both northwards and southwards.

Those who submitted to him, and did as he would have them do, increased and prospered, and saw good days; but all who stood in his way were wrecked on the sea and perished, for the Wind-Gnome was on his side.

So things quickly went from good to better with him. What was to him a fair wind was the ruin of all those who were in any way opposed to him. At last he became so rich and mighty that he owned every blessed trading-place and fishing-station in all Finmark, and sent vessels even as far as Spitzbergen.

===Chinese===

可巴尔杜恩只去一趟并不满足。他照旧前往，一趟趟从迪勒维克岩返回，船上装着绒鸭绒袋子，直堆到桅杆顶。

他买房屋，买船只；势力越来越壮大。

没过多久，南边北边整片整片的渔场，都归到了他名下。

凡归顺他、照他意思行事的人，家业日增月盛，日子过得兴旺；但凡拦他道的，无一不在海上船毁人亡——因为风精与他同在。

于是他的光景很快好上加好。于他是顺风的，对处处同他作对的人便是覆亡。到后来，他富极势大，全芬马克（Finmark）大大小小的商埠渔站无一不归他所有，船队甚至远航至斯匹次卑尔根（Spitzbergen）。

===Original===

Nobody durst sell fish up north without his leave, and his sloops sailed over to Bergen eighteen at a time.

He ruled and gave judgment as it seemed best to him.

But the magistrates thought that such authority was too much for one man to have, and they began to make inquiries, and receive complaints of how he domineered the people.

Next, the magistrates sent him a warning.

“But the right to rule lies in my rudder,” thought Bardun to himself.

===Chinese===

北边一带，没他准许谁也不敢卖鱼；他的单桅帆船开赴卑尔根，一次便是十八艘。

他统辖一方、裁断诸事，一切只凭自己的心意。

可长官们觉得，这般权柄握在一个人手里未免太过了，便着手查究，收受众人控告他如何作威作福、欺压民众的状纸。

随后，长官们向他发出了一道警告。

“然而统治之权，就握在我的舵里。”巴尔杜恩心中暗想。

===Original===

Then the magistrates summoned him before the tribunal.

Bardun simply whistled contemptuously.

At last matters came to such a pitch that the magistrates sailed forth to seize him in the midst of a howling tempest, and down they went in the Finmark seas.

Then Bardun was made chief magistrate, till such time as the king should send up another.

But the new man who came had not been very long in office there before it seemed to him as if it was not he but Bardun who held sway.

So the same thing happened over again.

===Chinese===

于是长官们传他上裁判所到案。

巴尔杜恩只轻蔑地打了个呼哨。

事态愈演愈烈，长官们终于在一阵咆哮呼号的风暴中扬帆出海，前来捉拿他，结果尽数沉没在芬马克的海里。

于是巴尔杜恩被任为首席长官，权且署理，只等国王另派新官上来。

可新官到任未久，便觉得在此地发号施令的不是自己，倒是巴尔杜恩。

于是同一出戏又原样重演了一遍。

===Original===

Bardun was summoned in vain before the courts, and the magistrates came forth to seize him and perished at sea.

But when the next governor was sent up to Finmark, it was only the keel of the king’s ship that came drifting in from the sea. At last nobody would venture thither to certain ruin, and Bardun was left alone, and ruled over all. Then so mighty was he in all Finmark that he reigned there like the king himself.

Now he had but one child, and that a daughter.

Boel was her name, and she shot up so handsome and comely that her beauty shone like the sun. No bridegroom was good enough for her, unless, perhaps, it were the king’s son.

===Chinese===

传巴尔杜恩到庭，也是白传；长官们出马前来捉拿他，又都葬身海上。

及至下一位总督再被派上芬马克，从海上漂回来的，只有国王座船的一截龙骨。到末了，再没有人肯上那儿去白白送死，巴尔杜恩便落得独自一人，统辖一切。那时他在整个芬马克权势之大，统治得俨然如国王本人一般。

如今他膝下只有一个孩子，而且是个女儿。

她名叫波埃尔（Boel），出落得秀美端庄，容光如太阳般耀眼。天下没有哪个新郎配得上她——兴许，除了国王的儿子。

===Original===

Wooers came from afar, and came in vain. She was to have a dower, they said, such as no girl in the North had ever had before.

One year quite a young officer came up thither with a letter from the king. His garments were stiff with gold, and shone and sparkled wherever he went. Bardun received him well, and helped him to carry out the king’s commands.

But since the day when he himself was young, and got the answer, “Yes!” from his bride, he had never been so happy as when Boel came to him one day and said that the young officer had wooed her, and she would throw herself into the sea straightway if she couldn’t have him.

In this way, he argued, his race would always sit in the seat of authority, and hold sway when he was gone.

While the officer, in the course of the summer, was out on circuit, Bardun set a hundred men to work to build a house for them.

It was to shine like a castle, and be bright with high halls and large reception-rooms, and windows in long rooms; and furs and cloth of gold and bright tiles were fetched from the far South.

===Chinese===

求亲的人从远方络绎而来，又都无功而返。人们说，她将有一份嫁妆，是北方姑娘从来不曾有过的。

有一年，一位十分年轻的军官带着国王的书信来到此地。他一身衣袍金线累累，走到哪里都熠熠生辉。巴尔杜恩殷勤接待，帮他办理国王交办的事项。

打从当年他自己年轻、从新娘口中得到那一声“愿意！”以来，他还从没有那样欢喜过——那天波埃尔来对他说，年轻的军官向她求了亲，她若嫁不成他，宁可立刻一头跳进海里。

他这般盘算：如此一来，他的血脉便可世代居于权位，在他身后依然执掌大权。

那年夏天，军官外出巡回办案，巴尔杜恩便征调一百名工匠，替他们起造宅邸。

宅子要造得辉煌如城堡：高敞的厅堂、宽阔的客室，长厅里开着一列列长窗；毛皮、金锦和明亮的瓷砖，都从遥远的南方采办运来。

===Original===

And in the autumn there was such a wedding that the whole land heard and talked about it.

But it was not long before Bardun began to find that to be a fact which was already a rumour, to wit, that the man who had got his daughter would fain have his own way also.

He laid down the law, and gave judgment like Bardun himself; and he overruled Bardun, not once nor twice.

Then Bardun went to Boel, and bade her take her husband to task, and look sharp about it. He had never yet seen the man, said he, who couldn’t be set right by his bride in the days when they did nothing but eat honey together.

But Boel said that she had wedded a man who, to her mind, was no less a man than her own father; and it was his office, besides, to uphold the law and jurisdiction of the king.

===Chinese===

到了秋天，那场婚礼办得轰轰烈烈，举国上下都听说了，都在谈论。

可没过多久，巴尔杜恩便发觉，先前只是传闻的事竟成了事实——也就是说，娶了他女儿的那个人，也偏要自作主张。

他也颁定规矩、裁断是非，俨然另一个巴尔杜恩；而且他压过巴尔杜恩，不止一回两回。

于是巴尔杜恩去见波埃尔，嘱咐她去规劝丈夫，还要即刻上心。他说，他还从未见过哪个男人，在新婚燕尔、整日价甜如蜜的日子里，不能被新娘子扳过来的。

可波埃尔说，她嫁的这个男人，依她看并不比她父亲逊色；何况他的职分，本就是维护国王的法律与司法。

===Original===

Young folks are easy to talk over, thought Bardun. One can do anything with them when one only makes them fancy they are having their own way. And it is wonderful how far one can get if one only bides one’s time, and makes the best of things. Whatever was out of gear he could very easily put right again, when once he got a firm grip of the reins.

So he praised everything his son-in-law did, and talked big about him, so that there was really no end to it. He was glad, he said, that such a wise and stately ruler was there, ready to stand in his shoes against the day when he should grow old.

And so he made himself small, and his voice quivered when he spoke, as if he were really a sick and broken-down man.

But it didn’t escape Boel how he slammed to the doors, and struck the stones with his stick till the sparks flew.

Next time the court met, Bardun was taxed to a full tenth of the value of all his property, according to the king’s law and justice.

===Chinese===

年轻人好说话，巴尔杜恩心想，只消让他们觉得自己事事随心，便什么都好商量。而且只要沉得住气、善处时势，天大的事也能办成。凡是脱了榫的地方，等他一旦把缰绳攥牢，都不难重新拨正。

于是女婿做的事他件件称赞，把他夸得天花乱坠，简直没完没了。他说，有这般贤明庄重的执政者守在那里，待他年老之日正好接他的位，他心里欢喜。

他就此把自己装得低微，说话时嗓音发颤，仿佛当真是个病弱衰颓之人。

可他怎样把门摔得砰砰作响，怎样拿手杖敲得石头火星四溅，波埃尔都看在眼里。

裁判所再度开庭，依照国王的法律与公道，巴尔杜恩被课以全部家产价值的整整十分之一。

===Original===

Then only did he begin to foresee that it might fare with the magistrates now as it had done formerly.

But all women like pomp and show, thought he, and Boel was in this respect no different to other people. And she was no daughter of his either, if she couldn’t keep the upper hand of her husband.

So he bought her gold and jewels, and other costly things. One day he came with a bracelet, and another day with a chain; and now it was a belt, and now a gold embroidered shoe. And every time he told her that he brought her these gifts, because she was his dearest jewel. He knew of nothing in the world that was too precious for her.

Then, in his most pleasant, most courtly style, he just hinted that she might see to it, and talk her husband over to other ways.

===Chinese===

直到这时，他才开始预见到：如今这些长官，怕要落得同从前那些人一样的下场。

可是女人个个爱荣华排场，他想，波埃尔在这上头与旁人并无两样。她若制服不住自己的丈夫，也就算不得他的女儿。

于是他给她买金饰、买珠宝，买各样贵重之物。今天携来一只镯子，明天送来一条项链；忽而是条腰带，忽而又是一只金绣鞋。每回他都对她说，送她这些礼物，是因为她才是他最珍贵的珠宝。普天之下，他知道再没有什么东西贵重得配不上她。

随后，他便以最和悦、最谦恭有礼的姿态，只轻轻点了一句：她不妨留意着些，把她丈夫劝上别的路子。

===Original===

But it booted him even less than before.

And so things went on till autumn. The king’s law was first, and his will was only second.

Then he began to dread what would be the end of it all. His eyes sparkled so fiercely that none dare come near him. But at night he would pace up and down, and shriek and bellow at his daughter, and give her all sorts of vile names.

Now one day he came in to Boel with a heavy gold crown full of the most precious stones. She should be the Queen of Finmark and Spitzbergen, said he, if her husband would do according to his will.

===Chinese===

然而这比先前更加无济于事。

事情就这样挨到了秋天。国王的法律居先，他的意志只得靠后。

他开始害怕这一切终将如何了局。他双眼凶光灼灼，没人敢走近他身边。到了夜里，他便踱来踱去，冲着女儿尖声喊叫、大声咆哮，把种种恶毒的名字加在她头上。

一天，他捧着一顶沉甸甸、镶满至宝的金冠进来见波埃尔。他说，只要她丈夫肯依他的意思行事，她就做芬马克和斯匹次卑尔根的女王。

===Original===

Then she looked him stiffly in the face, and said she would never seduce her husband into breaking the king’s law.

He grew as pale as the wall behind him, and cast the gold crown on the floor, so that there was a perfect shower of precious stones about them.

She must know, said he, that her father and none other was king here. And now the young officer should find out how it fared with them who sat in his seat.

Then Boel washed her hands of her father altogether, but she advised her husband to depart forthwith.

And on the third day she had packed up all her bridal finery, and departed in the vessel with the young officer.

===Chinese===

她却直僵僵地瞪着他的脸，说她决不肯诱劝丈夫去违犯国王的法律。

他的脸霎时白得如同身后的墙壁，把金冠掼在地上，掼得宝石如骤雨般在他们四周飞溅。

他说，她须知：在这地方称王的，不是别人，正是她父亲。如今，那年轻军官也该尝一尝，坐他这把交椅的人是何等下场。

波埃尔从此同父亲一刀两断，再不相干；只是她劝丈夫即刻动身离去。

到第三天，她已收拾好全部嫁时华服，随同年轻军官登船而去。

===Original===

Then Bardun smote his head against the wall, and that night he laughed, so that it was heard far away, but he wept for his daughter.

And now there arose such a storm that the sea was white for a whole week. And it was not long before the tidings came that the ship that Boel and her husband had sailed by had gone down, and the splinters lay and floated among the skerries.

Then Bardun took the rudder he had got from the Wind-Gnome, and stuck it into the stern of the largest yacht he had. He was God himself now, said he, and could always get a fair wind to steer by, and could rule where he would in the wide world. And southwards he sailed with a rattling breeze, and the billows rolled after him like mounds and hillocks.

Heavier and heavier grew the sea, till it rolled like white mountains as high as the rocky walls of Lofoten.

===Chinese===

巴尔杜恩一头朝墙上撞去。那天夜里，他放声大笑，笑声远传四方——可为着女儿，他哭了。

随即起了这样一场风暴，海整整翻了一个星期的白浪。不久便传来消息：波埃尔和她丈夫乘坐的那条船沉没了，碎裂的船板漂在礁岛群之间。

于是巴尔杜恩取出风精所赠的那柄舵，装上他最大的一艘快帆船的船尾。他说，如今他自己就是上帝了，随时都能得着顺风行船，在这茫茫世界上想统治哪里，便统治哪里。他乘着猎猎疾风一路向南，巨浪在他船后翻滚，宛如一座座土丘山岗。

海越涌越沉，翻腾起来如白色的高山，同罗弗敦（Lofoten）的岩壁一般高。

===Original===

It couldn’t well be less when he was to rule the whole world, cried he. And so he set his rudder dead southwards.

He never diminished his sail one bit, and worse and worse grew the storm, and higher and higher rose the sea.

For now he was steering right into the sun.

===Chinese===

他嚷道，他既要统治整个世界，这浪头自然小不了。说罢便把舵死死对准正南。

他一寸帆也不肯收；风暴愈来愈猛，海浪愈涌愈高。

因为此刻，他正径直朝着太阳驶去。

## Tug of War / 拔河

===Original===
For the last two or three days the weather had been terrific; but on the third day it so far cleared up that one of the men who belonged to the fishing station thought that they might manage to drag the nets a bit that day. The others, however, were not inclined to venture out. Now it is the custom for the various crews to lend each other a hand in pushing off the boats, and so it happened now. When, however, they came to the Fembøring, which was drawn up a good distance ashore, they found the oars and the thwarts turned upside down in the boat, and, more than that, despite all their exertions, it was impossible to move the boat from the spot. They tried once, twice, thrice; but it was of no use. But then one of them, who was known to have second sight, said that, from what he saw, it would be best not to touch the boat at all that day; it was too heavy for the might of man to move. One of the crew, however, who belonged to the fishing-station (he was a smart lad of fourteen), was amusing them all the time with all manner of pranks and tomfoolery. He now caught up a heavy stone, and pitched it with all his might right into the stern of the boat. Then, suddenly and plainly visible to them all, out of the boat rushed a Draug in seaman’s clothes, but with a heavy crop of seaweed instead of a head. It had been weighing down the boat by sitting in the stern, and now dashed into the sea, so that the foam spirted all over them. After that the Fembøring glided quite smoothly into the water. Then the man with second sight looked at the boy, and said that he should not have done so. But the lad went on laughing as before, and said he didn’t believe in such stuff. When they had come home in the evening, and the folks lay sleeping in the fishing-station, they heard, about twelve o’clock at night, the lad yelling for help; it even seemed to one of them, by the light of the train-oil lamp, as if a heavy hand were stretching forward from the door right up to the bench on which the lad lay. The lad, yelling and struggling, had already been dragged as far as the door before the others had so far come to their senses as to think of grasping him round the body to prevent him from being dragged right out. And now, in mid doorway, a hard fight began, the Draug dragging him by the legs, while the whole crew tugged against him with the boy’s arms and upper limbs. Thus, amidst yelling and groaning, they swayed to and fro all through the midnight hour, backwards and forwards, in the half-open door; and now the Draug, and now the men, had the most of the boy on their side of the doorway. All at once the Draug let go, so that the whole crew fell higgledy-piggledy backwards on to the floor. Then they found that the boy was dead; it was only then that the Draug had let him go.

===Chinese===
前两三天，天气一直狂暴得吓人；到第三天总算放晴了些，渔场上有个汉子琢磨着，这天或许还能勉强下海收一收网。可其余的人都不愿冒险出海。按老规矩，各船的人下水时总要彼此搭把手推船，这回也不例外。谁知等他们来到那条拖上岸搁得离水老远的芬伯艇（Fembøring）跟前，却发现桨和座板在船里翻了个底朝天；更邪门的是，任凭众人使尽气力，那船竟纹丝不动。他们试了一回，两回，三回；全不顶事。这时人群里一个出了名生有阴阳眼（second sight）的汉子开了腔：依他看出来的光景，这天最好压根儿别去碰那条船；它坠得太沉，不是人力挪得动的。偏偏船上有个渔场来的小子（十四岁，机灵得很），一直胡闹耍宝、插科打诨，逗得大伙儿直乐。这会儿他拎起一块大石头，使足了劲，直朝船尾砸去。说时迟那时快，只见一个德劳格（Draug）猛地从船里窜了出来，众人都看得清清楚楚：它穿着水手的衣裳，顶着的脑袋却是一蓬沉甸甸的海草。方才正是它坐在船尾把船坠住，此刻一头扎进海里，激起的水花溅了众人满身。打那以后，那条芬伯艇便顺顺当当滑进了水里。阴阳眼汉子瞧着少年，说他不该这么干。可那小子照旧嘻嘻哈哈，说这种玩意儿他才不信。傍晚他们回到家，渔场里的人们躺下睡熟了，到半夜十二点上下，众人忽然听见那小子声声喊救命；借着鱼油灯的光，其中一人甚至瞧见，一只沉甸甸的手仿佛正从门口一直伸到那小子躺卧的长凳上。那小子又叫又挣，已经一路被拖到门口，其余的人才算醒过神来，想起拦腰抱住他，别让他被整个拖出门去。于是在门道正中，一场硬仗开了场：德劳格拉住他的双腿，全船的人攥着他的双臂和上身，跟他往回对拔。就这样，在喊叫与呻吟声里，他们在半开的门中前前后后地拉锯摇摆，熬过了整个午夜时辰；一会儿是德劳格，一会儿是众人，各自把少年身子的大半截拽到自己那一边。冷不防德劳格撒了手，全船人登时仰面朝天，跌作一堆。这时他们才发现，少年已经死了；原来直到那时，德劳格才松开了他。
